    - **18 m:** fixoff REAL-ish **2/16** (post-gt median 14.0 m) vs fixon **2/16** → CLEAN FAIL-CLOSED; the fixoff "handoffs" are FOOLED phantom-handoffs onto the ~14 m stale cue (fixoff PHANTOM=9); recovery WEAK (R8: recovered-by-fix 2 / lost-by-fix 2, net 0).
    - **22 m:** fixoff REAL-ish **0/16** (post-gt median 19.5 m) vs fixon **1/16** → STRONG FAIL-CLOSED; the fix is FAIL-SAFE with ~no recovery (R8: recovered 1 / lost 0).
    - **Monotonic witness collapse — fixoff REAL-ish 12 → 2 → 0 across 15/18/22 m — is a clean dose-response demonstration of the ADR-0059 fail-closed bug, worse as the jam moves pre-acquisition.**
  - **The honest headline (a failure-mode FLIP, not a recovery).** The fix does NOT restore the comms-denied intercept: net recovery is ~0 at 18 m (R8: recovered 2 / lost-by-fix 2) and +1 at 22 m (recovered 1 / lost 0) — single-flight counts within run-to-run noise. BUT it converts fail-closed's WORST failure mode — being FOOLED into a phantom-handoff onto the frozen cue and flying at a 14–19 m ghost (fixoff fooled-handoffs 9 @18 m, post-gt median 14.0 m; 5 @22 m, post-gt median 19.5 m) — into FAIL-SAFE: age out the dead cue and abort cleanly (fooled ghost-handoffs 9→1 @18 m, 5→0 @22 m; PHANTOM fully eliminated at 22 m). The anti-phantom property HOLDS under jam. AND when fixon DID reach a camera-only handoff it hit well (post-handoff gt Pk 3/3, median 0.70 m @18 m; 1/1 at 1.76 m @22 m) — the camera-only terminal WORKS when it engages; the limit is that WITHOUT `--coast-search` it rarely reaches handoff (13–15/16 abort as `never`).
  - **Gate decomposition (separate the signal from mis-calibrated/control gates).** **G3a** (fail-closed witness) PASSES @22 m (REAL-ish 0, never 11 ≫ control) and fails @18 m only on a never-margin technicality: the 18 m collapse is FOOLED-PHANTOM-dominant, not rejection-dominant — the gate assumed fail-closed = rejection, but at 18 m it manifests as fooled-handoff (FLAGGED as a gate refinement, not a data problem). **G3b/G3c/G4** fail in both verdict sets = recovery didn't reach the pre-registered full-recovery bar — the true honest signal (fail-safe, not recovery). The CONTROL-side failures are NOT about the fix: **G5** (strict~r2 inertness) fails on a systematic −0.377 m SAFE-direction shift — median|d| 0.274 m PASSES the 0.7 m bar, but sign/Wilcoxon p≈0 (and handoff agreement 11/16 vs the 14 bar) reflect Gazebo/RTF nondeterminism vs the Jul-9 r2 baseline → "inert = byte-identical OUTPUT" is NOT literally true across separate full-Gazebo runs, only equal within ~0.3 m nondeterminism (the CODE-PATH byte-identity from the unit/pinned tests DOES hold). **G6b** fails only on an un-meetable PHANTOM==0 bar (active PHANTOM=4 vs strict's own 6/16 no-jam baseline — the noisy range-quality label again). **G6a** WARN: fallback engaged 0/16 under the realistic no-jam dropout cue — the fix stayed FULLY INERT (no spurious engagement, a GOOD safety property), though per the checker's own WARN this leaves control_active as WEAK evidence on always-on fallback behavior (the Markov-expected ~70–80% engagement never manifested).
  - **Caveats (read before quoting any number).** (1) Recovery counts are 1–2 flights — within noise; do NOT read 2-vs-1 as a recovery-vs-depth trend. (2) NO `--coast-search` was flown, so the 13–15/16 fixon non-handoffs are AMBIGUOUS between cue-gate failure and FOV/steering loss (checker R9 ambiguity note) → the recovery number is a LOWER bound. (3) Scoped to speed-12/gate-8 only — the checker's own header forbids generalizing beyond it. (4) The PHANTOM label CONFLATES range-channel-noise phantoms on good intercepts with fooled-ghost phantoms (e.g. the one surviving fixon PHANTOM @18 m delivered post-gt 0.56 m — a range-noise label, not a stale-cue ghost); decompose before reusing the label.
  - **What is now EARNED and defensible (vs what stays HELD).** "Works comms-denied" stays HELD as an INTERCEPT claim — the fix does not restore the intercept. EARNED: *"under a pre-acquisition cue jam the deployment config fails closed — fooled into phantom-handoffs onto the stale cue, 0/16 real intercepts @22 m; the ADR-0059 staleness fix converts this to FAIL-SAFE (ages out the dead cue, aborts cleanly, phantom eliminated at 22 m) — a validated anti-fooled SAFETY property. Full camera-only RECOVERY = fix + `--coast-search` (ADR-0015's WORST-tier dead-reckon-coast), the documented next arm (task #39), plus a `--cue-stale-horizon` tradeoff sweep (the 1.0 s horizon costs handoffs at shallow cutoffs — the 15 m premature-age-out)."*
  - **STATUS:** ADR-0059 = fail-closed DEMONSTRATED + fix VALIDATED-AS-FAIL-SAFE (not recovery); comms-denied intercept stays HELD; recovery follow-up = task #39. Artifacts: `logs/mc_adr0059_{control_strict,control_active,jam_fixoff_r15/r18/r22,jam_fixon_r15/r18/r22}.csv`; verdict transcripts `check_{15,18,22}m.txt` (session scratchpad). Date 2026-07-10.

## ADR-0060 — MEASURED FINDING (design-review variable #19): the pitched-down forward camera throws the horizon-level target to the TOP of the frame during the dash — ~35% of dash ticks the target is entirely ABOVE the FoV, and the first in-range detection currently threads a 0–6° sliver from the top edge with ~zero vertical margin. Naive "camera points below the target" framing is INVERTED; the sim UNDER-states the gap for real hardware.
- **What / method.** Design-review variable #19 asked whether the interceptor's fixed forward camera points off-target during the high-speed dash. Vehicle attitude is NOT in the flight CSVs (`m4_intercept.py` CSV writer logs only `psi_deg` yaw), but PX4 SITL ulogs carry `vehicle_attitude` at ~250 Hz on the sim clock. `scripts/experiments/dash_pitch_probe.py` (new, read-only, no sim runs) matches each mc_t21 run to its ulog, aligns ulog→CSV time via the altitude trace (residual 0.01–0.07 m), and computes per-tick pitch + the target's position in the camera frame from the full quaternion + `gt_cam`/`gt_tag` world points. Camera facts: ZERO mount tilt (boresight = body +x; the `x500_mono_cam`/`mono_cam` SDF poses have no pitch), HFOV 99.7°, 1280×960 → **VFOV 83.3° (±41.6° vertical half-cone)**.
- **Measured (32 runs — trackgate_weave12_r2 + track_jink12, dash=16 m/s `--dash-unclamp`).** DASH nose-down pitch: per-run median 16–42° (arm medians 27°/36°), **per-run max 42–52°** — at the full-accel tilt `atan(12/9.81)=50.7°` implied by the FPV bundle's `MPC_ACC_HOR_MAX=12` (under `MPC_TILTMAX_AIR=60`). The target LOS sits at the horizon (median −0.3°), so the pitched-down camera throws the target to the **TOP** of the image: median in-camera vertical 28–41° above boresight vs the 41.6° top edge. **Median ~35% (range 22–57%) of dash ticks have the target ENTIRELY above the FoV.** Detection confirms it bites: pooled 1,827 dash ticks ≤20 m, det rate 96% in-FoV → 86% top-edge band → **22% when the target computes above the edge**. **First in-range detection at median pitch −36°, target −38° to −41° vertical — a 0–6° sliver from the top edge; handoff currently succeeds by threading that sliver with essentially zero vertical margin.**
- **Sim honesty nuance (the gap is WORSE on real hardware).** This is ACCELERATION-TRANSIENT tilt — the dash ends at handoff ~10 m while still accelerating (achieved 9.3–16.6 m/s), and Gazebo's x500 has low aero drag, so a long steady 16 m/s cruise would settle near level. A real drag-limited quad sustains 20–30°+ pitch for the WHOLE cruise → on longer real dashes the target spends MORE time above a fixed forward camera, not less. So the sim UNDER-states this gap. (The velocity-setpoint abstraction is NOT hiding it — PX4 SITL genuinely tilts and the body-fixed camera genuinely renders it; the effect is real and already costing dash detections in-sim.)
- **Implications.** (1) Handoff works today but with ~zero vertical margin — fragile; likely a contributor to the "sparse-detection" dash flights (e.g. the v3 Phase-0/ADR-0058 low-coverage flights). (2) **HYPOTHESIS (unconfirmed, cheap to check):** the real target being squished/clipped at the top edge may make the ADR-0058/v3 MIS-LOCK phantom (v2 grabbing a big centered interior texture over the small edge target) MORE likely — a cross-link to the v3 eval; cross-check = correlate mis-lock frames with dash pitch. (3) **Remedy = a fixed UP-TILTED camera mount** (the FPV-quad convention) sized to cruise pitch — trades against ENGAGE-phase pointing, so it is a real airframe/guidance DESIGN DECISION, not a tuning knob; it feeds Stage-0 / deployment M-1..M-4 and the parent airframe design. (4) It may ALSO reduce phantoms if hypothesis (2) holds (keeps the target centered/larger).
- **Status / follow-up (task #35, sim-gated to idle).** Characterized. Next: an up-tilted-camera-mount A/B in sim (add a camera mount pitch, re-run the dash flights, quantify dash-detection recovery + handoff vertical margin), and the cheap mis-lock×pitch correlation cross-check on the v3 eval frames. Feed the resulting mount-angle into the deployment/Stage-0 design.
- **Date:** 2026-07-10. `scripts/experiments/dash_pitch_probe.py`; sources = the `flight_csv_path` CSVs + matched ulogs under `~/PX4-Autopilot/build/px4_sitl_default/rootfs/log/2026-07-09/`, camera SDFs under `~/PX4-Autopilot/Tools/simulation/gz/models/`.

## ADR-0061 — CLOSED (NULL / mild regression, task #28): the onboard seeker v3 maneuvering-domain fine-tune does NOT ship. It REGRESSES flight recall (v2 0.47→v3 0.19 maneuver, 0.59→0.18 line, worse on ALL 10 held-out flights, paired sign p=0.002) while acing its OWN static-grid val — the static far-range data did NOT transfer to the flight far-range problem. Phantom-rate is lower but UNDERPOWERED and merely a side-effect of v3 firing 5-20× less. A pre-results forward audit + 4 pre-registered scorer fixes correctly PREVENTED a false win. v2 stays deployed; detect-then-track (ADR-0058, 14/14) already holds the maneuver, so this null is benign.
- **What v3 was.** The ADR-0053/0055 ground-detector medicine ("train the deployment domain, split structurally, evaluate held-out") applied to the onboard detector's 12 m/s weave/jink domain (the ADR-0056/0057 phantom premise). Dataset: a 648-pose STATIC teleport grid (ranges 1.5–50 m, full 8-yaw aspect ring, ±25° bearing, `scripts/seeker/seeker_v3_capture.py`) ∪ 16 ride-along flights (weave/jink/line 6–15 m/s, seeds 1101–1116, `capture_pass_v3.sh`) ∪ legacy v1 renders + v2 capture; FLIGHT-level + held-out-RANGE split (`build_onboard_dataset_v3.py`); base `yolo11n.pt`, imgsz 640, 60 epochs, batch 16, seed 0 — single variable vs v2 = the dataset. Plan `docs/seeker_v3_dataset_plan.md`.
- **Dataset (audited clean, so the null is real, not a data bug).** Train 2512 (1382 pos / 1130 neg), val 1188 (218 / 970). Leakage audit PASS two independent ways (the builder's own + `verify_split_v3.py`): flight-level + held-out-range disjoint, legacy train-only, val flights EXACTLY {capv3_03/04/08/10}, val grid ranges EXACTLY {3,18 m}, ZERO eval frames. Labels validated (grid v2-vs-label IoU 0.977 + 0/648 geometry out-of-tol; flight close-range center-err 5.9–9.7 px; ultralytics reported 0 corrupt over all 3700 imgs). **Phantom MINING yielded mined_neg=0** — v2 NEVER fires post-mask (conf 0.25) on the ~3300 empty negative frames; the phantom is a POSITIVE-frame MIS-LOCK (v2 locks a big interior texture INSTEAD of the small real target; 31 `mined_pos` co-visible frames tagged), not an empty-frame false-alarm.
- **VERDICT (frame-level, v2-vs-v3 on IDENTICAL held-out `evalv3` frames, deployed conf-0.25 + self-mask, deterministic offline; `logs/seeker_v3_eval/run_20260710T083822Z/`).** v3 does NOT ship:
  - **PRIMARY — held-out maneuvering-flight recall: REGRESSED.** Maneuver recall v2 **0.473 → v3 0.189** (clause_a COLLAPSE → G2 FAIL); the 15–30 m in-flight bucket v2 0.241 → v3 0.034 (WORSE, not the sought +0.10); per-flight recall paired sign test **v3 worse on ALL 10 flights (0 wins / 10 losses, p=0.002)**.
  - **G3 no-regression: FAIL** — line-6/9 recall v2 0.588 → v3 0.176.
  - **Phantom (secondary, G1 UNDERPOWERED):** v3 lower (v2 9/1414 → v3 1/1414 phantom-frames; per-flight sign p=0.008) BUT (a) UNDERPOWERED (v2 count 9<10, Wilson CIs overlap) and (b) a SIDE EFFECT of v3 firing 5–20× LESS overall (n_raw/frame 0.2–0.4 → 0.01–0.10), not a targeted fix. MIS-LOCK (oversized off-target on a positive frame) = 0/74 for BOTH on these frames.
  - **Static-grid probe (SUPPORTING only — v3's OWN val split; v2 is ZERO-SHOT there; ASYMMETRIC, NEVER "generalization"):** v3 aces it — 3 m 0.79→1.00, 18 m 0.96→0.97. This proves v3 is a WORKING detector that generalizes WITHIN the static-grid domain; it did NOT transfer to flight.
- **Nuance + likely mechanism.** Independent spot-check: at CLOSE range (≤6 m) v3 ≈ v2 (both match 14/25) — v3 is NOT broken (and the ONNX decode is fine; it aces the grid). The collapse is at MID/FAR flight range (off-axis crossing, top-edge-clipped per ADR-0060). LIKELY MECHANISM (hypothesis, NOT ablated): static-grid-dominated positives (~504 static vs ~347 flight in the train split) + heavy banked-attitude negatives that RESEMBLE the low-signal flight positives → v3 learned the clean static-target appearance and over-suppressed flight/banked frames → flight mid/far recall collapse. The static far-range grid was the WRONG domain for the flight far-range problem.
- **Cross-links.** (1) The MIS-LOCK and the range-channel fragility are the SAME "wrong box" cause: an oversized off-target box yields a garbage/negative range (capv3_06 handoff `r_hat=-104 m`) — fixing LOCALIZATION fixes both the phantom handoff and the range channel feeding Vc/freeze (ADR-0059). (2) ADR-0060's dash-pitch finding (the pitched-down forward camera throws the target to the TOP edge / clips it) is a candidate cause of the transfer failure — exactly the hard flight far-range positives v3 missed; the cheap mis-lock×pitch cross-check (ADR-0060 follow-up) can test it.
- **The forward audit EARNED ITS KEEP (methodology win, the reportable process result).** A pre-RESULTS forward audit found `verdict()` would have declared a FALSE WIN on noise. Four fixes were pre-registered BEFORE any scoring: (1) G2 clause_b +0.10 counts ONLY if the bucket Wilson CIs are disjoint OR a per-flight paired sign test is significant (the bucket is only ~29 frames → a +0.10 "win" = ~3 correlated frames); (2) G3 tolerance = max(0.02, 1/n_gt) OR CI overlap, and the upside never fails (dropped `abs()`); (3) WIN also requires clause_a (no maneuver-recall collapse) + null-branch print made consistent with WIN; (4) SPAN footgun — recalibrate the v3 span sidecar FIRST (fit **0.9382**), assert `MARKERLESS_SPAN_M` unset, fresh timestamped out. WITHOUT these, the grid probe (v3 1.00/0.97 vs v2 0.79/0.96) would have LOOKED like a v3 win; the fixes correctly made FLIGHT recall the primary and clause_a caught the regression. `eval_seeker_v3.py --self-test` all green.
- **Decision / implication.** **v3 NOT adopted; `drone_finetuned_v2.onnx` stays the deployed `MARKERLESS_NN_WEIGHTS`.** The null is benign: detect-then-track + `--handoff-cue-gate 8` (ADR-0058, 14/14 post-latch at 12 m/s weave) already holds the maneuvering camera-only terminal, and Option B (markerless flies for real) is ratified — v3 was incremental hardening, not load-bearing. A v4, IF pursued, needs FLIGHT-POSITIVE-DOMINANT training (not static-grid-heavy) + gentler/less-target-similar negatives, likely paired with an UP-TILTED camera mount (ADR-0060) so flight far-range positives aren't top-edge-clipped; else the detect-then-track path carries the maneuvering fix. Result is CONDITIONAL on this one ADR-0057-nondeterminism render draw; the n=10 flight sign test is near-unpowerable and is not leaned on.
- **Honesty.** Grid 3/18 m = v3's OWN val split (checkpoint + span optimized on it), v2 zero-shot → asymmetric, never framed as symmetric generalization. Phase-0 forensics ran on `evalv3_01/02` = TWO of the six gate flights (disclosed, not "eval-adjacent"). `gt_*` used OFFLINE only to score/manufacture labels; v3 (had it shipped) re-earns the per-tick no-cheat audit. Datasets + weights gitignored (T17 precedent); `yolo11n.pt` at repo root untracked, not committed.
- **Process note.** The first fine-tune launch was REAPED at epoch 9 by the background-command harness (no crash; ultralytics had checkpointed); recovered by relaunching a `setsid`-detached daemon (`scripts/seeker/train_daemon_v3.py`) that RESUMED from the epoch-9 checkpoint and ran 60 epochs to completion — immune to the reaping (the detachment the sim captures use). Also caught + cleaned 2 orphan `gz sim` servers from a teardown gap (capture_pass teardown kills `gz sim` but not the `gz_x500_mono_cam` make/build chain); `frames_seen` normal across all flights confirmed NO double-publish corruption.
- **Date / artifacts.** 2026-07-10. Report `logs/seeker_v3_eval/run_20260710T083822Z/{eval_report.txt,summary.json}`; weights `scripts/seeker/weights/drone_finetuned_v3.{pt,onnx}(+.calib.json)` (gitignored); new tooling `scripts/seeker/{seeker_v3_capture,capture_pass_v3,grid_capture_v3,v3_onnx_infer,phantom_forensics_v3,verify_grid_labels_v3,build_onboard_dataset_v3,verify_split_v3,train_daemon_v3,phase4_eval_v3}` + `scripts/eval_seeker_v3.py`.

## ADR-0062 — TWO audit-driven guidance-correctness fixes SHIP (tasks #37/#38): (A) full-attitude derotation of the LOS bearing, and (B) a noise deadband on the past-CPA range-increase breakoff. Both Fable-reviewed, both gated; FIX-A A/B-VALIDATED as help-or-neutral at dash tilt (7/8 seeds tighter, 0 worse); FIX-B ships INERT (default-off, byte-identical) pending its own gate.

- **Context.** The deep + forward overnight audits surfaced two independent guidance-path defects. Audit-3 **H1 (structural):** the terminal LOS azimuth was computed `lambda = psi + beta` — vehicle yaw plus the target's camera-optical horizontal bearing — which is correct ONLY when the camera boresight is level. At the measured 27–36° dash pitch (ADR-0060) that inflates the azimuth by ~1/cos(pitch) (12–24% gain error) and, under roll, mixes the target's VERTICAL off-boresight component into the azimuth — corrupting lambda and lambda_dot, the pro-nav input. Deep-audit **#37 (CONFIRMED correctness):** the past-CPA range-increase breakoff counted any strict range rise as a step, so sensor/quantization jitter (~cm) could ratchet a false breakoff streak on a target that had NOT actually receded.

- **FIX-A — full-attitude derotation (`derotate_bearing_lambda`, #38).** Rotate the camera ray camera(optical)→body(FRD)→NED with the full own-state attitude quaternion, then `lambda = atan2(east, north)`. Uses the 3D camera vector (carries the elevation the bearing alone throws away, so the roll cross-coupling is captured); atan2 is scale-invariant so range magnitude is irrelevant. New `track_attitude_quaternion` subscription (MAVSDK `attitude_quaternion`, own-state EKF — same honesty basis as position; re-earns the no-cheat audit). **Identity guarantee:** with a pure-yaw quaternion the chain reduces to `psi + beta` EXACTLY (the vertical component lands entirely in NED-down, never touching north/east), so the validated LEVEL M3/M4/M5 results are preserved to the bit — only tilt introduces a (correct) change. Fallback to `psi + beta` during the brief startup window before the attitude stream is up (nothing regresses to worse-than-before). Pinned by `tests/test_bearing_derotation.py` (245-case identity grid to <1e-9 + pitch/roll oracle cross-check).

- **FIX-A — A/B RESULT (the validation).** Paired weave A/B, n=8, master-seed 42, deployment config (`--track --handoff-cue-gate 8`), same 8 cue-seeds each arm. **ON (derotation): Pk@2.5 8/8, median 1.897 m. OFF (baseline): Pk@2.5 8/8, median 2.172 m.** Paired: derotation tighter on **7/8 seeds, worse on 0** (median delta −0.161 m; sign test p≈0.035). No hit-rate regression (8/8 both). VERDICT: correcting the bearing at dash tilt CONSISTENTLY tightens the maneuvering intercept — modest (~0.16 m, near the ~1 m run-to-run noise floor) but directionally clean and mechanism-backed. `check_m4` PASS (pronav 0.444 m — FIX-A is a no-op at level, as the identity guarantees). Per-tick no-cheat audit stays PASS.

- **FIX-A — SCOPE BOUNDARY (reviewed, ruled SOUND).** The correction is applied to the pro-nav command LOS + the PIP/fused target-track vectors; the acquisition/handoff GATES stay yaw-only (`psi + beta`). Fable review confirmed this is safe: the gates trip at 6–8 m LOS separation while the tilt-induced azimuth error is ~1–3 m at dash range — the margin holds, and forcing the gates through the derotation would be a larger, unvalidated change. Gate derotation is a tracked follow-up (#40), not a blocker.

- **FIX-B — breakoff deadband (`update_range_increase_streak`, #37).** Extracted the inline streak logic into a PURE function (exhaustively unit-testable, `tests/test_breakoff_deadband.py`, 8 cases). A step counts as a RISE only when `meas_range > last_fresh_range + deadband`; a within-deadband creep HOLDS the streak without advancing the baseline (so quantization jitter can't ratchet a false streak, yet a real slow post-CPA recession — measured 0.04–0.11 m/detection on legitimate breakoffs — still fires within one extra detection). New flags `--breakoff-deadband-m` (0.0), `--breakoff-min-rise-m` (0.0), `--breakoff-max-range-m` (None). **DEFAULT is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pre-fix inline logic** (deadband 0 ⇒ empty creep band ⇒ every step is a strict rise or a reset, exactly as before). Ships INERT: the tuned non-zero values are a separate gate before they turn on, so this commit carries the mechanism with zero behavior change and zero regression risk.

- **Decision / why now.** BOTH ship this commit. FIX-A is on (A/B-validated help-or-neutral, identity-preserving at level, review-approved). FIX-B is present-but-inert (default byte-identical) so the mechanism + tests land under review while the tuning gate runs separately — avoids a second churn of `m4_intercept.py` and keeps the audit findings from going stale uncommitted. Both are honesty-legal (own-state attitude only; `gt_*` untouched).

- **Follow-ups (#40, tracked, NOT blockers).** (1) Up-tilt mount: `derotate_bearing_lambda`'s optical→FRD permutation hardcodes ZERO mount tilt — if the ADR-0060 up-tilt mount A/B (#35) is adopted, compose the constant mount rotation in (loud code comment added at the permutation). (2) M7 slant-range + gate derotation. (3) Attitude CSV columns (quaternion/roll/pitch) for offline reproducibility of the derotation — LOW, deferred. (4) FIX-B tuning gate to pick + turn on non-zero deadband/min-rise/max-range.

- **Honesty.** FIX-A consumes only camera measurements (`meas_xyz`, `bearing_rad`) + own-state EKF attitude (`quat`, `psi`) — the same basis as position used everywhere else; no ground truth; per-tick no-cheat audit re-run PASS. FIX-B is a pure kinematic filter on the camera range channel. `gt_*` remains scoring/logging only.

- **Date / artifacts.** 2026-07-10. Fixes in `scripts/m4_intercept.py` (`derotate_bearing_lambda`, `track_attitude_quaternion`, `update_range_increase_streak` + breakoff flags) and `scripts/seeker/detect_track.py` (`MARKERLESS_TRACK_BORDER_MARGIN_PX` border-coast, supporting). Tests: `tests/test_bearing_derotation.py`, `tests/test_breakoff_deadband.py` (suite 102 passed / 2 skipped). A/B logs: `logs/mc_fixA_on_weave.csv`, `logs/mc_fixA_off_weave.csv`.

- **Addendum (2026-07-10, jink n=16 post-fix): FIX-A holds AND improves on the jink path.** The adopted config (`--track --handoff-cue-gate 8`) had only been batch-validated on weave (r2, ADR-0058); FWD-A4 flagged the post-fix jink re-run as pending. Flown now on HEAD (post-FIX-A), n=16, master-seed 42: **pooled Pk@2.5 = 16/16, handoffs 14/16, median 1.535 m, max 2.280 m** (`logs/mc_t21_trackgate_jink12.csv`, harness `scripts/mc_t21_trackgate_jink16.sh`). This BEATS the pre-fix ADR-0058 jink baseline (pooled 15/16, camera-terminal 14/15) and is tighter than the r2 weave headline (median 2.11 m) — FIX-A's tilt derotation helps on jink as it did on weave. Per-tick no-cheat audit re-run PASS on a jink flight (a/b/c pass; own-state attitude only). NOTE (#33): this is post-FIX-A data and must NOT be pooled with the pre-FIX-A r2 weave arm for a Pk claim (different code version — see the #33 A2-FRESH note).

- **Addendum (2026-07-10, task #39) — the RECOVERY arm is a NULL: `--coast-search` does NOT restore the comms-denied intercept; the binding constraint is onboard PERCEPTION, not guidance.** Per the pre-registration (`docs/adr0059_recovery_preregistration.md`), the recovery hypothesis was fix + `--coast-search` (ADR-0015): on cue staleness dead-reckon the dash to the predicted acquisition basket then run a bounded ±20° yaw sweep to reacquire camera-only. Flown at the r18 pre-acquisition cutoff (n=16, master-seed 42, `logs/mc_adr0059_jam_fixon_coast_r18.csv`). Coast-search ENGAGED on every jam flight (the `[s2] COAST-SEARCH: cue stale (age 1.00s > 1.0s) … dead-reckoning` latch fired; verified in the run logs — this is NOT a plumbing no-op). RESULT vs the pre-registered thresholds: **REAL-ish handoffs 1/16 (need ≥11; no-coast fixon baseline was 2/16), headline joint 1/16, `never`=12** — a clean **NULL**, nowhere near recovery.
  - **Mechanism (diagnosed, the reportable finding).** On **10/16 flights the camera never detected the target at all** during the coast+sweep (`first_det_range_m` empty, coverage ≈ 0). Coast-search's *guidance* worked — it dead-reckoned to the basket — but the *camera* could not reacquire at the 15–21 m coast range. Two compounding, known perception limits: (1) the markerless NN's far-range recall is weak (the same weakness that killed the v3 retrain, ADR-0061), and under jam the detect-then-track seeker must ACQUIRE from scratch with no cue seed; (2) the dash pitches nose-down (ADR-0060) so the horizon-level target (measured elevation ≈ −1° here) sits above the down-pointed camera boresight — the LATERAL yaw sweep cannot bring a vertically-out-of-frame target into view. This **confirms ADR-0059's own headline** ("perception AVAILABILITY, not terminal guidance accuracy, is the binding constraint once the cue is degraded"): a guidance behavior cannot fix a perception-availability problem.
  - **r22 not flown (honest, not selective-stopping).** The mechanism — onboard perception availability at the coast range — is CUTOFF-INDEPENDENT (the dash pitch and the NN's far-range recall do not depend on the jam range). A deeper pre-acquisition jam (r22) makes the perception problem strictly harder, so it can only reproduce the r18 NULL; it adds no new information about whether coast-search recovers. Recorded so a reviewer can request the confirming dose-response point if wanted.
  - **Verdict / decision.** "Works comms-denied" **STAYS HELD** as an intercept claim — the recovery is not achieved. `--coast-search` (guidance) is NOT the fix; the recovery effort is **re-scoped from guidance to PERCEPTION**: an up-tilted camera mount (task #35, keeps the target in-frame during the pitched dash) + a better far-range detector are the load-bearing levers, and only after them would a coast-search (extended to a pitch-inclusive search) plausibly reacquire. This makes the ADR-0060 up-tilt mount load-bearing for BOTH nominal dash acquisition AND comms-denied recovery.
  - **Honesty.** Coast-search + the staleness fix are camera-only + dead-reckoned own-state (same legality as `--warm-handoff`); `gt_*` scoring/logging only; the per-tick no-cheat audit remains legal on the coast path. Artifacts: `logs/mc_adr0059_jam_fixon_coast_r18.csv`, pre-registration `docs/adr0059_recovery_preregistration.md`, verdict via `scripts/check_jam_mc.py` (coast arm scored as `--fixon`).

## ADR-0063 — FLIGHT COMPUTE DECIDED (council 2×B + 1 staged-C dissent; BUILDER-RATIFIED "B"): the real interceptor flies the sim-validated MARKERLESS NN seeker — the ADR-0058 detect-then-track stack on a Raspberry Pi 5 + Hailo-8 NPU on a 5–7" frame — NOT classical CV, NOT the AprilTag (the tag was only ever the sim stand-in for "a reliable target lock exists"); bench-gated on Stage-0 (~$257 cart + Hailo ~$70–110), NOTHING claimed until sustained Hz/latency are measured on the compute that actually flies
- **Context.** The sim-to-real design review (`docs/design_review_sim_to_real_2026-07-10.md` §4) surfaced an internal contradiction: the Stage-0 plan benches a **Pi 5** but the Stage-2 BOM flies a **Pi Zero 2 W** running the parent design's classical frame-differencing CV — so the headline bench-Hz number would never transfer to the compute that flies, and the 149 g-AUW 2.5" airframe cannot lift the Pi 5 it benched. Review Action 4: decide which ALGORITHM on which BOARD flies the terminal seeker, log the ADR, then bench THAT configuration — a one-way door for Stage-2 money, so a 3-member decision council ran (independent seats: inference latency/capability; risk/sim-to-real-transfer/solo-buildability; cost/weight/airframe-power). Meanwhile the sim had already settled the ALGORITHM at the top evidence tier: ADR-0056/0057 proved naive markerless perception throws high-confidence phantoms at 12 m/s maneuver (confidence INVERTED — no threshold saves it), and ADR-0058's detect-then-track (NN acquires once, cv2 CSRT follows at frame rate, NN re-validates every 8 frames, cue-gated handoff) recovered it to **14/14 post-handoff camera-terminal with zero phantom handoffs**. Per GOALS.md the AprilTag is the sanctioned SIM STAND-IN for the seeker's target lock; what flies was always the open question.
- **Options considered.** **(A)** Pi Zero 2 W + classical frame-differencing CV (~$18, ~10 g — the parent-BOM plan). **(B)** Pi 5 + Hailo-8 NPU running the ADR-0058 YOLO+CSRT detect-then-track stack (~$200–240 compute; forces a 5–7" class frame). **(C)** middle paths the council was invited to construct (Pi-5-CPU-only NN; a staged split flying the validated AprilTag seeker first with the NN held at bench tier; zero-weight NPU boards e.g. RV1106/RKNN class).
- **Decision (builder-RATIFIED, verbatim: "B it is imperative we make markerless work i know its possible").** **B.** The real interceptor's terminal seeker is the markerless NN detect-then-track stack — the SAME configuration ADR-0058 validated in Gazebo — on **Pi 5 (8 GB) + Hailo-8** (8L ~$70 floor, 8 ~$110 preferred), carried by a **5–7" class frame** (the seeker sets the airframe, not the reverse — consistent with ADR-0012's "2.5-inch is a fantasy… deployable = 7-inch class"). Classical frame-differencing is REJECTED as a flight seeker in any configuration (3/3 council seats). The Pi Zero 2 W survives only as a possible seeker-less logger/bring-up rider on the cheap Stage-2 build, never as the terminal seeker. The parent 2.5" BOM is SUPERSEDED as the seeker carrier. Hardware-side, nothing moves until the builder orders the Stage-0 cart — **the purchase stays builder-gated** (overnight report §2 decision B); this ADR is the doc half of task #34.
- **Why (each line traces).** (1) **A's algorithm is already empirically dead IN THIS REPO** — the classical prototype was built and evaluated: all 40 of its CSV "detections" were own-airframe edge wedges emitted at conf=1.0, a standing high-confidence FALSE bearing, verdict "never ship classical as a standalone bearing source" (`docs/seeker_prototype_results.md`, `scripts/seeker/classical_seeker.py`); frame-differencing has no identity channel, so it cannot do the cue-validated seed or the every-8-frames IoU re-validation that fixed the maneuver phantom (ADR-0058), and its ego-motion compensation structurally degrades under a translating camera with parallax (`docs/seeker_design_brief.md`) — exactly the ADR-0060-measured 42–52° nose-down dash that puts TERRAIN, not sky, behind the target. (2) **A's board cannot run the validated seeker by ~20–50×, not a tuning margin**: Pi-5-CPU YOLOv8n measured ~13 fps (ADR-0015 #1) and the ground NN ran 0.85–1.25 s/frame on a DESKTOP CPU (T19b); CSRT is CPU-bound (no USB accelerator rescues a Zero 2 W, 4×A53 @1 GHz / 512 MB). (3) **The latency budget closes only with the NPU**: terminal LOS rates 32–58 deg/s (ADR-0014/0023) against a ~0.41 s median terminal window (ADR-0023) — 100–200 ms of stacked latency is 25–50% of the window; Pi 5+Hailo is ~35 fps / ~29 ms end-to-end at the EXPECTED tier (ADR-0016 — sourced but UNMEASURED, hence the bench gate). (4) **Only B flies the sim-validated configuration** (ADR-0058: weave 3/16→10/15, jink 3/15→14/15, headline 14/14 / 0 phantoms; the jink result shows the win is temporal DENSITY, not only phantom rejection — any board that can't hold the ~14 Hz cadence the stack assumes, `MARKERLESS_TRACK_FRAME_DT_S=1/14`, forfeits the validated result). (5) **Program cost beats line-item cost**: A risks ~$530 of Stage-2/3 airframe spend validating an unvalidated seeker the sim predicts will false-lock ("no BOM dollar fixes perception risk — only a measurement retires it"); B's true delta is ~$200–240 of compute plus the airframe class ADR-0012 already prescribed.
- **Council reasoning + dissent (3 independent seats, transcripts in session task logs).** **Latency seat → B** (confidence high on rejecting A, medium-high on B-with-Hailo vs Pi-5-CPU-only): argued (1)–(4) above; flip condition = a Stage-0 bench showing Pi-5-CPU-only sustains ≥14 Hz through thermal soak (then drop the Hailo and lighten). **Cost/weight seat → B** (high): even under a cost-first lens A cannot run the only validated seeker; Pi 5+Hailo+cooler+GS-cam+BEC ≈ 135–165 g at 10–15 W doubles a 2.5"'s AUW (consistent with `docs/fpv_fidelity_design.md`), so B's "bigger airframe" is not new money — it is the already-ratified ADR-0012 plan; power (~15 W) is ~4% of a pack over a flight, weight not energy binds. **Risk/buildability seat → C, the DISSENT** (staged split: fly the validated AprilTag seeker on minimal compute for Stage-2/3, keep the NN as a Stage-0/1 bench-validated artifact, defer NN flight behind the bench + a larger-airframe decision): GOALS.md sanctions the tag as the honest stand-in; B is over-committed for a first-time solo builder (bigger frame + Hailo toolchain + first hardware build = highest stall probability); NN flight is not REQUIRED for the portfolio claim. The dissent's own stated flip condition: *"if the builder's portfolio priority is explicitly 'markerless NN flies in reality'… then B on a 5–7 inch frame becomes the honest choice — but only after Stage-0 bench numbers."* The builder's ratification is exactly that priority, verbatim — so the dissent RESOLVES to B **with its substance kept intact**: the bench gate precedes all airframe money, nothing is claimed until measured, and the tag-seeker arc remains the documented fallback (the detect-then-track architecture + guidance transfer regardless of detector, `docs/nn_transfer_plan.md` §1) if the bench fails the NN. All 3 seats independently rejected A.
- **Sim-to-real bridge (what transfers, and the one honest swap).** The sim validated, at Gazebo tier: the guidance ladder (M0–M5, pursuit→pro-nav, Monte-Carlo), the markerless NN seeker itself (v2, the post-M5 arc), the ADR-0058 detect-then-track terminal, the cue-gated handoff, and the ADR-0062 FIX-A attitude derotation. The flight compute runs the **SAME stack** — same NN-seed + CSRT-follow + re-validate cadence, same gates, same knobs. The tag→NN swap — the one honest simplification GOALS.md licensed — was already UNWOUND in-sim (the markerless seeker replaced the tag and re-earned the no-cheat audit); what remains between sim and reality is real optics/blur, real backgrounds, real compute timing, and thermals — precisely the quantities Stage-0/1 are scoped to measure. The ground cue rig is SEPARATE compute (ADR-0016's split, two physical nodes) — this ADR decides only the air node.
- **Stage-0 bench gate (the load-bearing condition — nothing is claimed until it runs).** Cart ~$257 (Pi 5 8GB ~$130 official-reseller not marketplace, OV9281 global-shutter cam + ~100° wide M12 lens, active cooler/PSU/SD, + the $15 yaw-ramp jig the review moved into this cart) **+ the Hailo-8 as an explicit add (~$70–110)** — bench the configuration that FLIES, per review Action 4 (`docs/stage0_bench_plan.md`, review §6). Must measure: sustained END-TO-END NN+CSRT chain Hz + latency through a ≥10-min thermal soak (review gap row 3, critical — all sim timing constants assume ~14 Hz never measured on target hardware), CSRT loop time explicitly (review G7: absent from the prior bench plan; CSRT alone plausibly exceeds the 71 ms frame budget), and detection through the yaw-ramp at the sim's 32–58 deg/s terminal LOS rates. The gate cuts BOTH ways: Pi-5-CPU-only sustaining ≥14 Hz post-soak → drop the Hailo and lighten the airframe; the chain failing ~14 Hz even WITH the Hailo → the tracker must change and the sim-validated config must be RE-EARNED in Gazebo before flight (that reopens the algorithm, not just the board).
- **Honest open risks (the sim already exposed every one of these — the compute choice does not retire them).** (1) **Far-range NN recall is the weak link**: v2's held-out flight recall is 0.47 maneuver / 0.59 line, the v3 retrain to fix it was a NULL/regression (ADR-0061), and in the comms-denied recovery arm the camera never detected the target at all on 10/16 coast flights at 15–21 m (ADR-0062 #39 addendum). (2) **The dash pitch throws the target above the FoV** (ADR-0060): ~35% of dash ticks the target is entirely above frame at the measured 27–52° nose-down; first in-range detection threads a 0–6° sliver from the top edge; the sim UNDER-states this for real drag-limited hardware — the up-tilt mount A/B (#35) is load-bearing for BOTH nominal acquisition and any comms-denied recovery, and its adopted angle must be composed into FIX-A's mount rotation (ADR-0062 follow-up #40). (3) **"Works comms-denied" stays HELD**: ADR-0059 earned a validated FAIL-SAFE (anti-fooled) property, not recovery, and the #39 coast-search arm was a NULL — the binding constraint is perception AVAILABILITY once the cue degrades, and no flight-compute choice fixes a perception-availability limit. (4) **Every B-side throughput number is currently Tier-3** (web/vendor: ~35 fps/~29 ms EXPECTED, ADR-0016 says so itself) — that is exactly what the bench gate exists to retire. (5) **Toolchain + license**: custom-model compile friction on the Hailo Dataflow Compiler is documented, and the deployed v2 weights are Ultralytics-AGPL — the Apache fallbacks (YOLOX / NanoDet-Plus, with Hailo Model Zoo recipes) are pre-identified in `docs/nn_transfer_plan.md` if either binds. (6) **Real backgrounds**: the 0/155-false-terminal-dets headline was earned in ONE empty Gazebo world (the G8 clutter/decoy batch is scoped, and the real-footage eval #30 awaits builder approval) — the NN has never seen a real photon.
- **Cross-links.** ADR-0012 (airframe class ruling), ADR-0016 (compute split + the latency tiers this decision inherits), ADR-0056/0057/0058 (the phantom problem and the detect-then-track stack that flies), ADR-0059 + ADR-0062 #39 addendum (comms-denied HELD; perception-availability limit), ADR-0060 (dash-pitch/FoV), ADR-0061 (v3 NULL — far-range recall stays open), ADR-0062 (FIX-A derotation + the mount-tilt composition follow-up). Docs: `docs/design_review_sim_to_real_2026-07-10.md` (§4 flight-compute decision, §6/Action 4), `docs/stage0_bench_plan.md`, `docs/nn_transfer_plan.md`, `docs/overnight_report_2026-07-10.md` (§2B, §5 — file retired 2026-07-10, ADR-0066; recover via `git show e6f06d3:docs/overnight_report_2026-07-10.md`), `docs/seeker_prototype_results.md`, `docs/seeker_design_brief.md`.
- **Date:** 2026-07-10. Council ran overnight (3 independent seats, transcripts in session task logs); builder ratified same day ("B it is imperative we make markerless work i know its possible"); ADR written as the doc half of task #34 — the Stage-0 cart order remains the builder's action.

## ADR-0064 — Pk claim HARDENED to n=72: the adopted config clears **Pk ≥ 95% (95% CI) at a 2.8 m proximity radius**; 98.6% point at 2.5 m. The resume line's Monte-Carlo backbone (task #33).

- **Context.** The resume line ("validated via Monte-Carlo miss-distance analysis") and the ratified ≥95% Pk goal (ADR-0025 proximity-fuse metric) needed a DEFENSIBLE confidence interval, not a point estimate. The prior headline (r2, n=16, 16/16) only supports a two-sided-95% Clopper-Pearson lower bound of **79.4%** — n far too small for a "≥95%" claim (`scripts/stats_hardening_options.sh` sized this: n=72 all-clean → LB 95.0%). Complication: FIX-A (ADR-0062, full-attitude LOS derotation) shipped mid-session and CHANGES the weave terminal at dash tilt (A/B: 7/8 seeds tighter), so pooling new flights with the PRE-FIX-A r2 arm violates the byte-identical-pooling requirement (ADR-0062 addendum). Resolution: fly a fresh, homogeneous, POST-FIX-A campaign.
- **Method.** 72 post-FIX-A weave flights on the adopted deployment config (`--track --handoff-cue-gate 8` + dash-16 / early-handoff / cue-velocity / dash-unclamp / fuse-midcourse, 12 m/s). Composition: 4 fresh sub-arms (`mc_deployment_arm.sh --path weave --n 16`, master-seeds 1042/2042/3042/4042 = 64 flights) + the 8 `fixA_on` weave flights (seed 42, byte-identical post-FIX-A config, all clean). Flown ONE arm at a time at idle, n≤16 per background command (under the ~51-min reaper), 120 s cooldowns. Proximity metric (ADR-0025); Clopper-Pearson two-sided 95% (α/2 = 0.025), computed exact via a pure-Python binomial-tail bisection (neither venv ships scipy — a `stats_hardening_options.sh` assumption corrected here).
- **Result — Pk vs radius (pooled n=72; median 1.784 m, mean 1.618 m, max 2.757 m):**

  | R (m) | Pk@R | point | CP-LB (95%) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1.5 | 25/72 | 34.7% | 23.9% |
  | 2.0 | 50/72 | 69.4% | 57.5% |
  | **2.5** | **71/72** | **98.6%** | **92.5%** |
  | **2.8** | **72/72** | **100%** | **95.0%** ← ≥95%-CI bar cleared |
  | 3.0 | 72/72 | 100% | 95.0% |

  Fresh-64 alone reaches 94.4% CP-LB at 2.8 m; pooling the 8 clean `fixA_on` flights lifts it to exactly 95.0% (DISCLOSED — the point estimate is 98–100% either way).
- **The single sub-2.8 m miss is terminal noise, not a failure mode.** seed-3042 run 3, miss **2.757 m** — 0.26 m over the 2.5 m line — **handed off cleanly** (`handoff=1`), i.e. a wide terminal-dispersion draw, NOT a phantom or a fail-to-handoff. Across n=72 the adopted config has zero handoff/phantom failures; the only cost of the tighter 2.5 m radius is ordinary terminal dispersion.
- **Honesty.** (1) The proximity-fuse Pk is RADIUS-parameterized (ADR-0025): quote "Pk ≥ 95% **at R=2.8 m**", never a bare "95% Pk". (2) Weave-path + 12 m/s specific; NEVER pooled across paths/speeds (ADR-0025) — jink is separately 16/16 (ADR-0062 addendum). (3) The pooled-72 leans on the 8 `fixA_on` flights to cross 95.0% at 2.8 m; fresh-64 alone is 94.4% — disclosed. (4) Per-tick no-cheat audit legal (FIX-A is camera-only + own-state; re-verified this session). (5) This is the CUED deployment config (no jam); "works comms-denied" stays HELD (ADR-0059).
- **Decision.** The defensible portfolio Pk claim for the adopted config (weave, 12 m/s) is: **"Pk ≥ 95% (95% CI, n=72 all-clean) at a 2.8 m proximity radius; 98.6% point / 92.5% CI-LB at 2.5 m."** Portfolio surfaces adopt this radius-explicit framing (no bare "95% Pk"). Closes task #33's Pk-language question.
- **Date / artifacts.** 2026-07-10. `logs/mc_pk72_weave_s{1042,2042,3042,4042}.csv` + `logs/mc_fixA_on_weave.csv`; harness `scripts/mc_deployment_arm.sh` (A2-fresh path of `scripts/stats_hardening_options.sh`). Supersedes the r2 n=16 79.4%-LB framing.

## ADR-0065 — DESIGN IDEA (builder, 2026-07-10): ADAPTIVE / active camera tilt — tilt the camera UP in the dash for an earlier lock, return toward horizon at dash-end. The upgrade over the FIXED up-tilt mount (#35), pointed straight at the ADR-0060 pitch gap and the ADR-0059 comms-denied recovery NULL.

- **Context.** The dash pitches nose-down 27–36° (ADR-0060), throwing the horizon-level target ABOVE the fixed forward camera's FoV — ~35% of dash ticks the target is out of frame, and the first in-range detection threads a 0–6° top-edge sliver with ~zero vertical margin. The fixed up-tilt mount A/B (task #35 — now FLOWN, ADR-0067: FoV gap closes but small paired gain + uncompensated terminal cost, no mount adopted) tests ONE compromise mount angle against this. And the comms-denied recovery NULL (ADR-0059 / task #39) failed for EXACTLY this reason: under a pre-acquisition cue jam the coast-search reached the basket but the camera never reacquired (10/16 flights, zero detections) — the binding constraint was PERCEPTION AVAILABILITY (target out of the down-pitched FoV), not guidance.
- **The idea (builder's words, paraphrased).** Instead of a fixed mount, an **ADAPTIVE / active camera tilt**: tilt the camera UP during the dash to counter the nose-down pitch (keep the target framed → EARLIER lock), then return the camera toward horizon-level as the dash ends / the terminal begins (keep the target framed as the geometry flattens, "as best it can, given the airframe is still tilted"). It TRACKS the actual vehicle pitch across the trajectory, where the fixed mount can only pick one compromise angle.
- **Options.** (A) **Fixed up-tilt mount** (#35) — simple, cheap, robust, no moving parts, one compromise angle (best at one point of the dash, wrong elsewhere). (B) **Adaptive / active tilt** — a servo-tilted camera or a small gimbal that follows the pitch; better coverage at BOTH the dash and the terminal; BUT adds weight, cost, power, actuation latency, a control loop, and a mechanical failure mode.
- **Decision — DEFERRED, design-captured (not built).** Sequence: (1) finish the FIXED up-tilt A/B (#35) — it bounds the fixed-mount ceiling and is already flying; (2) if the fixed mount helps but isn't enough, **sim-test the adaptive schedule** — drive the camera pitch as a function of the vehicle pitch (or a scheduled ramp: up in dash → toward level at handoff) and re-fly the up-tilt-style A/B, reporting acquisition recovery (first_det_range_m, coverage, FoV margin) and, with the #40 mount-compose applied, the terminal miss. The adaptive tilt is a live **candidate to un-HOLD the comms-denied recovery** — it supplies the perception-availability the coast-search alone could not.
- **Honesty / cost framing.** Fixed mount = the cheap Stage-0 default; adaptive tilt = a Stage-later hardware upgrade (servo/gimbal on the Pi). Do NOT claim the adaptive tilt works without a sim A/B — it is an idea to test, not a result.
- **Cross-links.** ADR-0060 (dash-pitch/FoV finding), ADR-0059 + task #39 (comms-denied recovery NULL, perception-availability limit), task #35 (fixed up-tilt A/B), task #40 (mount-compose de-rotation needed for the compensated terminal metric). Memory: `adaptive-camera-tilt-idea`.
- **Date.** 2026-07-10.

## ADR-0066 — Doc declutter: retire non-load-bearing portfolio + one-off audit docs to git history

- **Context.** Builder directive (2026-07-10): docs an autonomous session never loads
  were bloating context; portfolio/interview material will be regenerated at project
  end by a dedicated agent, so carrying draft copies through every session in the
  meantime is pure overhead with no compounding value.
- **Decision.** `git rm` 9 files: the 4 portfolio-facing docs (`docs/interviewer_prep.md`,
  `docs/portfolio_bullets.md`, `docs/portfolio_visuals.md`, `docs/WRITEUP.md`) and the
  5 absorbed one-off reports (`docs/audit_deep_2026-07-10.md`,
  `docs/audit_forward_2026-07-10.md`, `docs/audit_pipeline_frames_2026-07-10.md`,
  `docs/overnight_report_2026-07-10.md`, `docs/review_track_fix_delta_20260709.md`)
  whose findings live entirely in `docs/audit_findings_tracker.md` (the canonical
  ledger) and the ADRs above. All 9 are recoverable in full at git hash `e6f06d3`
  (the commit immediately before this removal) via `git show e6f06d3:<path>`. Inbound
  references patched same-commit: README, NEXT.md, `docs/demo_plan.md`,
  `docs/t25_storyboard.md`, the audit tracker's header, `.gitignore`'s evidence
  comment, `scripts/check_jam_mc.py`'s two provenance citations (docstring +
  printed banner), and this file (ADR-0058's `review_track_fix_delta_20260709.md`
  citation and ADR-0063's overnight-report cross-link).
- **Why.** Context budget is a real, finite resource for an autonomous session that
  reloads project docs every turn; these 9 files were never load-bearing for a build
  decision (their substance is already captured in the tracker + ADRs), only for a
  future portfolio-writing pass. Removing them is zero information loss — git history
  is the durable archive — and a direct win for every session's signal-to-noise.
- **Date.** 2026-07-10.
## ADR-0067 — #35 up-tilt mount A/B FLOWN (4 arms × n=8, paired seeds): mechanism CONFIRMED — a fixed up-tilt closes the ADR-0060 dash-FoV gap (dash-above-FoV 32% → 0%) — but the paired acquisition gain is SMALL (+2–3 m, the interim "+7.6 m" read was baseline-confounded) and the uncompensated terminal cost is dose-dependent; NO mount adopted until #40 mount-compose flies

- **Context.** ADR-0060 measured ~35% of dash ticks with the target above the FoV;
  ADR-0059's comms-denied recovery NULL is perception-bound (no reacquire at
  15–21 m). #35 asks whether a fixed up-tilt camera mount recovers that
  availability. Arms: 0°/15°/25°/35° up-tilt, paired seeds (master 42),
  weave/12 m/s, adopted config, n=8/arm. Harness `scripts/uptilt_ab_arm.sh` +
  `scripts/uptilt_make_variants.py` (model-shadow symlink swap, self-cleaning);
  analysis `scripts/experiments/uptilt_ab_analyze.py`. Evidence:
  `logs/mc_uptilt_weave12_up{00,15,25,35}.csv` + `logs/uptilt_ab_analysis_20260710.txt`
  (all committed with this ADR).
- **Result — mechanism (the availability question).** Dash-in-FoV 61% (up00) →
  90/87/93% (up15/25/35); dash-above-FoV **32% → 0%** at every tilt — the
  ADR-0060 gap CLOSES. Paired mechanism check confirms the swap took:
  top-margin +9.6°/+18.6°/+27.5° (expected ≈ +15/+25/+35°).
- **Result — acquisition range, and the confound caught.** Paired first-detection
  deltas vs the up00 control: **+2.92/+1.83/+2.26 m median** — real but SMALL.
  The interim note "first-detection 14.3 → 21.9 m (the mount WORKS)" compared
  up15 against the OLD r2 baseline (14.25 m); the sweep's own up00 control reads
  20.46 m, and the analyzer's paired check shows the old baseline detecting
  **−5.31 m later than up00 on identical seeds** — the old baseline's conditions
  differ systematically from this harness. Honest gain = the within-sweep
  +2–3 m, NOT +7.6 m. (Statistics-before-verdicts; the interim claim is
  corrected on every surface in this commit.)
- **Result — terminal (worst-case by construction).** Flown UNCOMPENSATED (m4
  assumes a zero mount, so the boresight offset biases terminal LOS): miss
  median 1.81 → 2.42/2.59/2.79 m; Pk@2.5 8/8 → 4/8, 3/8, 2/8 as tilt rises.
  Expected penalty, NOT the design ceiling — per the ADR-0062 boundary note,
  #40 mount-compose gates any terminal verdict. n=8/arm: Wilson-CI language
  only; no binomial headline claims from this sweep.
- **Decision.** NO mount angle adopted. The availability win is real and is the
  recovery-relevant quantity, but whether it un-HOLDs ADR-0059 recovery is
  undecidable until (a) #40 compensation removes the terminal penalty and
  (b) a recovery re-run flies tilted+compensated. At this n and uncompensated,
  up15 shows most of the coverage win at the least terminal cost — noted as the
  leading candidate, not a choice. Adaptive tilt (ADR-0065, #46) gains standing:
  a fixed compromise angle buys availability at a terminal price adaptive
  scheduling would avoid.
- **Date.** 2026-07-10. (Chain `bska471mo` up00/up25/up35 + prior up15; shadow
  symlink verified removed post-chain; analyzed same session, builder away.)
- **Addendum (2026-07-10, same day — Fable review pass).** Two review catches folded
  in: (1) **`engBelow` (engagement-below-FoV) was omitted from the result readout and
  is NON-monotone:** up15 shows **12%** of engagement ticks with the target below
  frame — the WORST of all four arms (up00 0%, up25 1%, up35 0%) — plausibly part of
  the terminal-cost mechanism (tilting up loses the bottom of frame in the endgame)
  and a point FOR adaptive tilt (ADR-0065). Carry `engBelow` into the #40 re-fly
  metric set alongside miss/Pk. (2) **The −5.31 m baseline systematic is diagnosed,
  not just flagged:** the old r2 baseline (`mc_t21_trackgate_weave12_r2.csv`) is
  PRE-FIX-A — ADR-0064 already banned pooling with it for exactly this reason.
  Formal consequence: **cross-era acquisition-range comparisons are INVALID**
  project-wide; any acquisition claim must use within-sweep paired controls.
  Also pre-register the #40 re-fly bar BEFORE it flies: paired n≥8 vs up00,
  terminal-miss parity criterion, recovery reacquire count, `engBelow`, and add a
  per-seed sign count to `uptilt_ab_analyze.py` (it currently prints medians only).

## ADR-0068 — #40 MOUNT-COMPOSE SHIPS: `--cam-mount-up-deg` composes the fixed camera-mount rotation into FIX-A's full-attitude LOS derotation (byte-identical at the 0.0 default); the uncompensated error is diagnosed as ROLL-driven; the re-fly bar is PRE-REGISTERED before any arm flies

- **Context.** ADR-0067 adopted NO mount: the up-tilt's availability win is real but the
  uncompensated terminal cost is dose-dependent, and every terminal verdict was gated on
  #40 — composing the mount rotation into `derotate_bearing_lambda` (the loud TODO at the
  optical→FRD permutation, ADR-0062 follow-up list item 1). This ADR ships that
  compensation + the pre-registered re-fly plan.
- **The fix.** New m4 flag `--cam-mount-up-deg` (default 0.0 = OFF): after the
  optical→body permutation, pre-rotate the ray by the constant R_y(+mount) — in FRD a
  positive rotation about +y tips body-forward UP, matching the SDF variants' negative
  sensor pitch (uptilt_make_variants.py sign note). Guarded (`if mount_up_rad:`) so the
  default keeps the EXACT pre-#40 float path — byte-identical, pinned by a bitwise
  identity test. Startup fallback (no attitude quaternion) stays `psi + beta`, mount
  ignored (documented: no frame chain to compose into; level-vehicle error is the small
  1/cos compression). The yaw-only acquisition/handoff gates are UNCHANGED — the
  ADR-0062 scope boundary re-checked at 15°: the 2D camera-implied-position error at the
  8 m cue gate is ≤ ~1.5 m; margin holds; gate derotation stays a tracked follow-up.
- **Mechanism refined (new understanding, numerically verified).** The uncompensated
  azimuth error is ROLL-driven, not pitch-driven: a 15° mount offset is a VERTICAL
  off-boresight term, and roll couples vertical into azimuth — audit-3 H1's exact
  cross-coupling mechanism, now sourced by the mount instead of the target's elevation.
  Measured on the oracle fixture (target az 20°, up15): ~0.65° wings-level at ANY pitch,
  ~3.0° at 15° roll, ~6.5° at 30° roll. The weave terminal rolls constantly — this is
  why ADR-0067's terminal cost was dose-dependent while its acquisition (wings-level
  dash) barely suffered. Retro-read of the ADR-0067 evidence with the new per-seed sign
  counts: uncompensated up15 was worse on **8/8 seeds**, median PAIRED delta **+0.84 m**
  (the +0.61 m in ADR-0067 is the delta-of-medians — a different statistic; the
  pre-registration binds the paired one to remove metric-shopping room).
- **Tests / gates (all sim-free, all green pre-commit).** Round-trip oracle: forward-
  generate the optical ray through an INDEPENDENT matrix chain (attitude + mount),
  derotation recovers the true inertial azimuth to <1e-9 over a grid incl. mounts
  0/15/25/35/−10°; bitwise byte-identity of the 0.0 default; roll-driven-error fixture
  (composed exact, zero-mount off by >2° at 30° roll); startup-fallback pin; bench
  self-check gains the MOUNT case (mirrored in `--bench`). `tests/test_bearing_derotation.py`
  13/13; full `scripts/run_tests.sh` ALL GREEN.
- **Harness.** `mc_deployment_arm.sh --m4-extra "FLAGS"` appends AFTER the r2-mirrored
  argv prefix (disclosed deviation, echoed in the plan print — the r2 provenance is
  preserved verbatim). `uptilt_ab_arm.sh --compensate` flies the re-fly: refly CSV names
  (`mc_uptilt_refly_weave12_up{00,15c}.csv` — ADR-0067 evidence never overwritten, and a
  --go guard now refuses ANY existing out-CSV), explicit `--cam-mount-up-deg 0` on the
  control (argv-symmetric, byte-identical by the guard). `uptilt_ab_analyze.py` now
  prints per-seed deltas + sign counts for miss / first_det_range / engBelow (the
  ADR-0067-addendum mandate; smoke-tested against the flown evidence).
- **Pre-registration.** `docs/adr0067_refly_preregistration.md` commits WITH this ADR,
  BEFORE any arm flies: stage 1 = up00 vs up15c, n=8 paired, parity bar (median paired
  miss delta ≤ +0.30 m AND worse ≤5/8; FAIL at ≥7/8 worse or ≥ +0.84 m), acquisition
  direction check, engBelow watch metric, validity gates (shadow mechanism check,
  machinery-stability tripwire vs the ADR-0067 up00, per-tick no-cheat audit). Stage 2 =
  tilted+compensated coast-search jam re-test, thresholds verbatim from
  `docs/adr0059_recovery_preregistration.md`, plus the availability mechanism bar
  (camera-never-detected ≤4/16 vs baseline 10/16). Only RECOVERY DEMONSTRATED un-HOLDs
  "works comms-denied", scoped.
- **Review (5-lens adversarial workflow, each finding refutation-verified; ran on
  Fable subagents before the mid-session Fable-limit switch to Opus).** 13/15 findings
  survived verification; ALL FIXED before this commit. Load-bearing catches: (1) **the
  Stage-1 validity gate 0 was diagnostically INVERTED** — because the analyzer adds the
  mount analytically and `vert_body` is pose-independent, a FAILED shadow swap reads
  d(top_margin) ≈ +mount EXACTLY with d(first_det) ≈ 0 (the naive "expected PASS"),
  while a WORKING swap reads d(top_margin) BELOW +mount (flown +9.6/+18.6/+27.5) with
  positive d(first_det); gate + analyzer text corrected, the invalid signature is now
  auto-flagged. (2) **control selection was CLI-order-dependent** — two mount-0 arms
  (the ride-along legacy up00) could silently re-base the PRIMARY parity read to the
  cross-era control (INVALID per the ADR-0067 addendum); analyzer now prefers the
  `refly` control and WARNs. (3) **n=16 INCONCLUSIVE bars were undefined** (the /8 sign
  bars don't scale) → α-matched n=16 thresholds + a terminal rule pre-registered now.
  (4) **Stage-2 "thresholds VERBATIM / no new goalposts" was false** in two places
  (comparator rebased fixon→coast-NULL loosening PARTIAL by one flight; the
  never-detected ≤4/16 bar is new) → both disclosed, PARTIAL scoped as cross-sweep
  suggestive. (5) **the absolute up/down mount sign wasn't pinned** — a coordinated
  flip across impl+oracle passed 13/13; added a physics anchor (level-ahead target
  through an up-mount lands below optical center, y_opt>0) that fails the coordinated
  mutation (verified). Plus: per-arm compensation-aware caveat (the blanket
  "UNCOMPENSATED" footer would have been false on committed re-fly evidence), med|Δ|
  tripwire stat, dropped-pair attrition surfaced, harness OUT-collision preflighted for
  all arms. Two findings correctly REJECTED (the +0.61/+0.84 statistic — already
  pre-empted; the stdout-log provenance — durable via the timestamped batch log).
- **Honesty.** The compensation consumes the static mount angle (an airframe config
  constant, known a priori) + own-state EKF attitude + camera measurements — no `gt_*`;
  `audit_per_tick.py` reads the logged `lambda_deg` (no recompute) and stays valid;
  no CSV schema change.
- **Date.** 2026-07-10. Code+tests+pre-registration in this commit; re-fly results land
  as an addendum.

- **Addendum (2026-07-10, task #40 — the mount-compose RE-FLY FLEW; STAGE-1
  VERDICT = FAIL on strict parity, but compensation RECOVERED most of the
  terminal cost).** Per `docs/adr0067_refly_preregistration.md` (pre-registered
  BEFORE the fly): up00 refly control vs up15 compensated (`--cam-mount-up-deg
  15`), n=8 paired, master-seed 42, `logs/mc_uptilt_refly_weave12_up{00,15c}.csv`
  + `logs/uptilt_refly_analysis_20260710.txt`.
  - **Validity gates.** (1) Shadow swap TOOK: up15c d(top_margin) +8.3° (below
    +15) with d(first_det) +4.07 m — the CORRECTED (review-fixed) valid
    signature, analyzer-flagged. (2) Machinery-stability tripwire PASS: fresh
    refly-up00 vs the ADR-0067 up00 reproduces to sub-noise (miss med|Δ| 0.065 m
    ≤ 1.0 m; first-det med +0.60 m ≤ 2 m) — the harness is stable, differences
    are real. (3) No-cheat audit: (a)/(b) PASS and the gt-leak (d) advisory is
    within the honest range on every arm → NO ground-truth leak. The (c)
    cmd-follows-camera correlation drops below its level-calibrated 0.7 on
    TILTED arms (uncompensated up15 0.437; compensated up15c 0.654 — compensation
    IMPROVES it), a confirmed tilted-terminal-GEOMETRY effect (both level arms
    pass, gt-leak normal), NOT a cheat and NOT compensation-specific. Recorded as
    a geometry-sensitivity of audit (c) for tilted mounts; honesty spine intact.
  - **PRIMARY — terminal parity (up15c − refly-up00, paired miss).** Per-seed
    +0.18 +1.02 +0.12 +0.49 +0.14 +1.31 +0.09 −0.02: **worse on 7/8 seeds**,
    median paired delta **+0.158 m**, **Pk@2.5 8/8**. Pre-registered bars: PASS
    needs worse ≤5/8 AND median ≤+0.30; FAIL is worse ≥7/8 OR median ≥+0.84.
    **7/8 worse ⇒ FAIL** (strict parity NOT achieved — a small, directionally-
    consistent residual remains). BUT vs the uncompensated up15 (ride-along,
    8/8 worse, median +0.82 m, Pk 4/8): **compensation cut the median penalty
    ~5× (+0.82 → +0.16 m) and RESTORED Pk@2.5 4/8 → 8/8.** So compensation
    WORKS — it removes most of the ADR-0067 terminal cost — it just does not
    reach statistical parity (still a ~0.16 m, within-noise-magnitude but
    sign-significant bias, carried mostly by 2/8 seeds compensation didn't fully
    fix).
  - **SECONDARY — acquisition retained:** up15c d(first_det_range) med +4.07 m
    vs refly-up00 (further = earlier detection), positive on 7/8 — the
    availability win holds under compensation (dash-above-FoV 35% → 0%, in-FoV
    58% → 89%). **WATCH — engBelow:** up15c 0% (vs uncompensated up15 12%) on
    the analyzable engage flights (denom noise flagged), no bottom-of-frame cost
    in this sweep.
  - **DECISION (per the pre-registered FAIL rule).** NO fixed +15° mount
    adoption on this n=8 strict-parity FAIL. The residual terminal bias + the
    fixed-angle compromise STRENGTHEN the case for #46 adaptive camera tilt
    (ADR-0065), which buys the availability without committing to one fixed
    angle. Compensation itself is VALIDATED and SHIPPED (ADR-0068): it is the
    prerequisite that makes any tilted config flyable, and it recovered the
    terminal cost from Pk 4/8 to 8/8. Stage 2 (tilted+compensated comms-jam
    recovery re-test) may still fly — the availability question stands
    independently of nominal-terminal adoption (pre-registration §Stage-2).
  - **Honesty.** Own-state attitude + static mount constant + camera only; no
    `gt_*`; audit spine intact (gt-leak normal, (a)/(b) pass); the (c) tilted-
    geometry sensitivity is documented above, not waved away.

- **Addendum (2026-07-10, #40 STAGE-2 — the tilted+compensated comms-jam RECOVERY
  re-test is a NULL, and it RE-SCOPES the recovery limit from FoV-availability to
  FAR-RANGE NN DETECTION).** Per the pre-registration (`docs/adr0067_refly_
  preregistration.md` Stage 2): the ADR-0059 #39 coast-search jam arm re-flown at
  r18, n=16, master-seed 42, WITH the up15 shadow + `--cam-mount-up-deg 15`
  (`scripts/stage2_tilt_recovery_arm.sh`; evidence
  `logs/mc_adr0059_jam_fixon_coast_r18_tilt15.csv`). Coast-search VERIFIED engaged
  (12 `cue stale -> dead-reckoning` latches — a real recovery test, not a plumbing
  no-op); `--cam-mount-up-deg 15` in all 16 flights; shadow removed post-run.
  - **Result vs the pre-registered bars.** REAL-ish handoffs (RH) **3/16** (need
    ≥11/16 for RECOVERY; #39 coast NULL was 1/16, so ΔRH = **+2/16**, below the
    +4/16 PARTIAL floor) → **NULL**. Scored by `check_jam_mc.py` (tilt coast as
    `--fixon`): G3b REAL-ish 3/16, 1 PHANTOM, 12 never.
  - **MECHANISM metric (the load-bearing one) — the availability claim is
    REFUTED in the recovery regime.** Camera-never-detected during coast =
    **12/16** (bar ≤4/16; #39 baseline 10/16) — the tilt did NOT reduce
    never-detected (12 vs 10, no improvement, within noise). The 4 flights that
    did reacquire detected only at **7.6–9.2 m** (near the 10 m handoff range),
    not the earlier reacquisition the availability win would predict.
  - **The re-scoping (the reportable finding).** #39 was AMBIGUOUS between two
    compounding recovery limits: (a) FoV geometry (the down-pitched dash throws
    the target above frame) and (b) far-range NN recall (acquire-from-scratch
    under jam at 15–21 m). The up-tilt DEMONSTRABLY fixes (a) — proven nominally
    (ADR-0067: dash-above-FoV 32%→0%, first-det +4 m) — yet recovery stays NULL
    and never-detected stays ~12/16. **Therefore the binding recovery limit is
    (b), far-range NN DETECTION, NOT the dash-pitch FoV.** Consistent with
    ADR-0061 (v3 far-range-recall retrain = NULL) and ADR-0063 open-risk #1.
    (Contributing nuance: during coast-search the attitude is less nose-down than
    the committed cued dash, so a dash-tuned up-tilt points less optimally there
    — but the dominant signal is that even successful detections happened only at
    close range, a recall limit, not a pointing one.)
  - **Verdict / claim state.** "Works comms-denied" **STAYS HELD** — recovery not
    achieved. The perception-availability lever (camera pointing: fixed #35 OR
    adaptive #46) is NOT the comms-denied recovery fix; the lever is a better
    FAR-RANGE DETECTOR. #46 adaptive tilt remains valuable for NOMINAL acquisition
    (the availability win is real there) but this NULL shows it would not un-HOLD
    recovery by itself. This closes the ADR-0059 → #35/ADR-0067 → #40 recovery
    arc: perception AVAILABILITY (FoV) is solved by the tilt; perception
    SENSITIVITY (far-range recall under jam) is the remaining hard blocker.
  - **Honesty.** Own-state + static mount + camera only; no `gt_*`. The tilted-
    camera audit-(c) geometry sensitivity (ADR-0068 Stage-1 addendum) applies to
    this arm identically; gt-leak advisory normal; honesty spine intact.

## ADR-0069 — Accel-predictive guidance (APN / OGL-ZEM / PIP-with-accel) ASSESSED against the builder's "2 m is too much": it is the WRONG lever — the weave miss is ~84% a fast-crossing KINEMATIC FLOOR (acquisition-range / ZEM-at-handoff bound), only ~0.27 m avg is the maneuver term, and the terminal APN rejection still holds. Build the one legal experiment (`--dash-accel-lead` on the CLEAN fused cue) as a unit-test-gated question-closer; put the real effort on ACQUISITION RANGE (adaptive tilt #46).

- **Context.** Builder (2026-07-10 eve): "2 m is too much — how much is the algorithm predicting the end location from the target's ACCELERATION / derivatives, beyond the current-velocity trajectory? Look up industry standard, implement if possible." A 5-agent research/design workflow (industry-standard research + code+history assessment + real-CSV miss decomposition + reviewed design + adversarial review; ran on Opus per the Fable-limit override) answered it.
- **What the code does (verified).** The camera-only terminal runs PLAIN pro-nav `a_cmd = N·Vc·λ̇` (N=5 FPV, m4:2380) — nulls LOS RATE, uses target VELOCITY implicitly, one lag late, NO acceleration. No filter carries an acceleration state (AlphaBetaFilter is x/xdot only, m4:843; TargetTracker pos+vel; solve_intercept_time is a constant-velocity quadratic). PIP (`--law pip`) leads on velocity only and is DEFAULT-OFF terminal (ADR-0011). Velocity-lead IS already on where the signal is clean: the DASH intercept-triangle solve + the `--warm-handoff` seed off the cue-emitted velocity. So the 2nd-derivative "aim beyond current velocity" idea is genuinely not implemented.
- **Industry standard (sourced).** The accel-predictive family is Augmented PN `a=N·Vc·λ̇+(N/2)·a_T` and its parent the Optimal Guidance/ZEM law `a=N'·ZEM/t_go², ZEM=r+v·t_go+½·a_T·t_go²` (the formal "predict from acceleration and aim there"). Against a WEAVE specifically: Zarchan (JGCD 1995, 10.2514/3.21492) — a sinusoid induces large PN miss; the fix is a weave-optimal ZEM carrying amplitude/phase/FREQUENCY, but it needs the freq to ±10% (JSR 10.2514/1.6459) and a raw seeker accel estimate can DIVERGE. Estimator ladder: α-β (CV, no accel) → α-β-γ / Kalman-CA-Singer (accel, "unimpressive under changing accelerations") → IMM w/ coordinated-turn mode (accepted best for maneuvering tracking).
- **The miss decomposition (the decisive part, real CSVs).** The ~2 m weave CPA is **ZEM-at-handoff dominated** (r²(ZEM@handoff, miss)=0.99, generalizing ADR-0023's straight-crosser finding). A **zero-acceleration** straight 12 m/s crosser already misses **~1.6 m** under the identical config (`logs/mc_final_line12.csv` 1.596 m vs weave12 ~1.62 m mean) — so **~84% (~1.6 m) is the fast-crossing kinematic floor present even at a_T≡0**, untouchable by ANY accel law. Only **~0.27 m avg (~0.76 m worst-direction +12, and the −12 phase HELPS by 0.33 m)** is the actual maneuver term (paired weave−line = +0.22 m; per-tick reconstruction = 0.27 m). The camera-only terminal is only **t_go ≈ 0.35 s**; nulling a ~2 m ZEM needs ~33 m/s² vs the ~8–13 m/s² the airframe delivers — **capacity-bound, not perception-bound**. Correction capacity ~ ½·a·t_go² and t_go is set by ACQUISITION/HANDOFF RANGE.
- **Does the old APN/PIP rejection still hold?** **YES for the terminal.** Detect-then-track (ADR-0058) + the fused cue (ADR-0044) improved mid-course custody / terminal detection DENSITY / phantom rejection — but NOT the terminal per-detection BEARING quality, which ADR-0056's AprilTag CONTROL proves is the binding limit (a clean-bearing seeker with the SAME plain pro-nav lands the weave 8/8 at 1.64 m → lead/accel is NOT the missing lever). No honesty-legal clean accel signal exists in the comms-denied terminal: the clean cue velocity is a PRE-handoff external comms signal, structurally latched off at handoff (ADR-0010 #5); the only camera-only velocity is the noisy α-β differentiation that already killed PIP (~7 m/s² noise); no filter carries accel.
- **The one legal experiment (design + review).** A ZEM/OGL accel-augmented lead applied in the MID-COURSE DASH ONLY, on the CLEAN cue-emitted velocity (`FusedTrack.vel_ned`, σ~0.5 m/s, 10 Hz, pre-handoff, honesty-legal): `aim = tpos + vt·t_go + ½·a_hat·t_go²`, a new `--dash-accel-lead` flag (default-off, byte-identical no-op), accel from an α-filter on the differenced cue velocity, horizon-capped (`--dash-accel-lead-max-tgo`), latched off at handoff with the cue. This is NOT the rejected case (different phase + clean signal). **Adversarial review verdict: GO-WITH-CHANGES, "it will NOT move the 2 m."** Primary expected failure = differentiating the σ=0.5 m/s, 10 Hz cue gives σ_a ≈ 7 m/s² (SNR<1 vs the 4.71 m/s² weave peak) — the same differentiate-noise mistake one derivative up; low-noise needs ~2 s averaging → ~90° phase lag → wrong sign at reversals. So: make the CHEAP UNIT TESTS the kill gate (require σ_a ≤ ~1.5 m/s² AND lag ≤ 1/8 weave period — expected to fail → kill BEFORE any sim spend); do NOT pre-commit n=72; keep the no-op identity + honesty audit + both-directions + "neither direction worse" bar.
- **Decision.** (1) Build `--dash-accel-lead` + the unit-test kill gate as an HONEST question-closer — implements what the builder asked, answers "did you aim at the acceleration?" with DATA (expected: a logged negative in the PIP/APN/Kalata family; portfolio-valuable). (2) The REAL lever for a tighter intercept is **ADR-0023 Tier-2 ACQUISITION RANGE** (earlier first-detection → bigger t_go; capacity scales t_go²) + mid-course delivered-ZEM — which is **exactly what the ADAPTIVE CAMERA TILT (#46, ADR-0065) attacks** (keeps the target in frame → earlier/cleaner detection). So the builder's two directives connect: adaptive tilt, not terminal accel-prediction, is the path to the tighter intercept. (3) Also reframe onto the ratified proximity-Pk metric (ADR-0025) — 2.08 m CPA is already Pk@2.5 8/8; "tighter" is partly a metric question.
- **Honesty.** The dash-accel-lead reads ONLY the cue-emitted velocity (pre-handoff external comms) + own-state; no `gt_*`; structurally off in the camera-only terminal. A per-tick audit will confirm the accel contribution == 0 on every post-latch tick on real flight logs.
- **Date.** 2026-07-10. Sources + full analysis: workflow `wf_8a3668ad-333` (Zarchan JGCD 1995 10.2514/3.21492; weave-freq JSR 10.2514/1.6459; APN US7446291B1; PPN-vs-TPN IEEE 53445; ZEM/OGL arXiv 2511.02526; bearing-only IMM DSP 2022; α-β-γ limits EURASIP 10.1186/s13634-015-0220-3). Repo: ADR-0009/0010/0011/0023/0044/0056/0058.

## ADR-0070 — Fixed-tilt + extended-handoff experiment: BOTH the tilt AND the "bigger t_go" premise are FALSIFIED — the ~1.5 m weave miss is TERMINAL-BEARING-NOISE-floored (ADR-0056), NOT acquisition-range/t_go-limited. Refines ADR-0069. The tilt (fixed OR adaptive) is NOT the tighter-intercept lever.

- **Context.** Builder pivot (2026-07-10 eve): try a cheap FIXED up-tilt before the adaptive gimbal. Pre-registered `docs/adaptive_tilt_prereg.md`; the ADR-0069 hypothesis was: tilt → earlier detection → EARLIER HANDOFF at longer range → bigger t_go → tighter CPA (capacity ~ ½·a·t_go²). 3 paired arms, weave 12 m/s, master-seed 42, n=8, in-project #40 camera shadow (NO PX4 swap). Evidence `logs/mc_tilt_{A_level,A2_level_ho15,B_up15_ho15}.csv`.
- **Result — the mechanism engaged but the miss did NOT respond.**
  | Arm | handoff range | CPA mean/median | Pk@2.5 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | A (level, default HO) | 6.46 m | 1.527 / 1.766 | 8/8 |
  | A2 (level, `--handoff-range 15`) | 13.54 m | 1.557 / 1.839 | 8/8 |
  | B (up15 + `--cam-mount-up-deg 15` + HO 15) | 13.59 m | **2.199 / 2.197** | 7/8 |
  - **Extending the handoff range WORKED mechanically** (6.46 → 13.54 m, t_go roughly DOUBLED) — but the CPA is UNCHANGED: paired A2 − A median **+0.016 m, 4/8 worse / 4/8 better**. **The extra t_go bought nothing.**
  - **The fixed tilt made it WORSE:** paired B − A **+0.478 m, worse 8/8**; and B − A2 (isolating the tilt, same handoff range) **+0.539 m, worse 7/8** — so the TILT, not the handoff extension, causes the loss. B has NO sub-1.5 m flights (A/A2 each had 3/8); its misses cluster high (1.76–2.57 m).
- **Mechanism (the reportable finding) — REFINES ADR-0069 and ADR-0023.** ADR-0023 framed the miss as capacity-limited (½·a·t_go² < delivered ZEM) and ADR-0069 named ACQUISITION RANGE (bigger t_go) as the tightening lever. This experiment DISPROVES that at the current operating point: doubling t_go left the miss flat, because the camera-only terminal is **BEARING-NOISE-limited (~1.5 m), not time/capacity-limited.** The extra active-guidance range (13.5 → 3.5 m) is flown on the noisy monocular box-center bearing, which cannot convert more time into a tighter null — exactly ADR-0056's binding limit (a clean-bearing AprilTag seeker with the SAME config already sits at 1.64 m). The tilt ADDS terminal pointing residual (the #40 roll-coupled/engBelow cost) over the now-LONGER camera terminal → it compounds → B worse. So: t_go and the tilt both move perception AVAILABILITY, which the ~1.5 m bearing-noise floor is insensitive to.
- **Decision / where the tighter-intercept lever actually is.** (1) **NOT the tilt** (fixed makes it worse; adaptive #46 does the same availability thing → same bearing floor → not worth pursuing for a tighter miss — the built gimbal stays default-off, its honest value is availability only, which ALSO didn't convert to comms-denied recovery, ADR-0068 Stage-2). (2) **NOT extending the handoff / t_go** (NULL). (3) **NOT accel-prediction** (ADR-0069). (4) **The real lever for < 1.5 m is TERMINAL BEARING QUALITY** — subpixel/centroid bearing vs the NN box-center (the ADR-0056 residual, a genuinely hard perception refinement) — OR the honest **metric reframe: 1.5–2 m is already Pk@2.5 8/8**; for a proximity-fuse interceptor with a lethal radius (ADR-0025) that IS a kill, so "tighter CPA" is largely the wrong objective. This is a strong portfolio arc: the guidance/acquisition levers are systematically eliminated → the binding limit is perception bearing quality.
- **Honesty.** Paired same-seed; in-project shadow (no PX4 touch); `gt_*` scoring only; the A2 control cleanly isolates the handoff-range knob from the tilt. n=8 screen — but the tilt's +0.48 m loss (8/8) and the handoff-extension NULL (4/8) are both well clear of the ~1 m single-flight noise for their signs.
- **Date.** 2026-07-10.

- **Addendum (2026-07-11, ADR-0071 A/B — subpixel bearing WORKS, tail-concentrated: it is the FIRST positive lever, improving the WORST-CASE miss + Pk robustness).** Paired A/B, weave 12 m/s, level camera, `MARKERLESS_TRACK_SUBPIXEL` 0 vs 1, pooled master-seed 42+43, n=15 valid pairs (1 dropped for a MAVSDK offboard-start NaN, unrelated). **Result:** paired mean CPA **1.767 → 1.608 m (−0.16 m)**, better on 9/15; the paired delta-MEDIAN is flat (−0.037 m) because the benefit is NOT a uniform median shift — it is **TAIL reduction**: box-center throws occasional high misses (max **3.04 m** = a Pk@2.5 MISS) that the centroid FIXES (**max 2.19 m**, that flight 3.04 → 2.01 m = miss→kill), lifting **Pk@2.5 14/15 → 15/15**. This is exactly the right shape for the ratified proximity-fuse metric (ADR-0025): the tail is what determines Pk. Mechanism confirmed: the box-center bearing jitters with the CSRT box edges (~box-width/2); the darkness-weighted centroid is edge-jitter-immune (unit test: box +5 px → centroid +0.00 px). Honesty-clean (camera pixels only; tracker/gating stay box-center). This is the FIRST lever to move the miss the right way after accel-prediction (ADR-0069), t_go/handoff-range (ADR-0070), and tilt (ADR-0070, worse) all failed — vindicating the ADR-0056/0070 "bearing quality is the binding limit" diagnosis. NOT yet the n=72 headline bar (ADR-0064): extending (master-seeds 44/45 → n≈31) to confirm the tail/Pk win before the full n=72.

- **Addendum #2 (2026-07-11, ADR-0071 A/B EXTENDED to n=31 — the subpixel "win" was SMALL-SAMPLE NOISE; the tighter-miss investigation is CLOSED: NO lever tightens the ~2 m weave miss, which is the practical seeker floor AND already a proximity-fuse KILL).** Extended the n=15 screen with master-seeds 44+45 (`logs/mc_subpix_{A,B}_s4{4,5}.csv`). **Pooled n=31 paired:** the promising n=15 mean (−0.16 m) REGRESSED as the sample grew — n=23 −0.075 m, **n=31 +0.062 m** (B marginally WORSE), sign 16 worse / 11 better, median delta +0.025 m. The apparent early win did not survive n. What marginally survives is TAIL-only + within noise: B's 90th-pctl 2.15 vs A 2.40 m and Pk@2.5 30/31 vs 28/31 (B clips two moderate 2.9–3.9 m outliers), but B's own worst flight is a catastrophic 7.66 m on master-seed-45 seed-5 — a HARD seed where the control ALSO fails (A 3.90 m, NO handoff), i.e. a phantom/hard-seed problem, not a clean subpixel failure. Likely mechanism for the null broad effect: the α-β lambda filter already smooths most of the per-frame box-center jitter, so a lower-jitter centroid adds little on top; the residual miss is the ADR-0023 kinematic floor + delivered ZEM, not per-frame bearing jitter.
