  - **DEFINITIVE ANSWER to the builder's "2 m is too much" (2026-07-10/11).** Rigorously eliminated EVERY lever: accel-prediction (ADR-0069, kill-gate NEGATIVE), longer t_go / handoff-range (ADR-0070, NULL), fixed & adaptive tilt (ADR-0070, WORSE), subpixel bearing (this, NULL/noise). **The ~1.5–2 m weave CPA is the practical FLOOR of this seeker architecture** — kinematically bounded at handoff (ADR-0023) and bearing-quality-bounded in the terminal (ADR-0056; even a clean AprilTag seeker sits 1.64 m), and box-center jitter is already filter-smoothed. **It is already a Pk@2.5 KILL (72/72 at n=72, ADR-0064; 8/8 here) — a proximity-fuse interceptor with a lethal radius DETONATES on target at 2 m (ADR-0025), so "tighter CPA" is the wrong objective; the intercept already SUCCEEDS.** A genuinely tighter miss needs a fundamentally different terminal seeker (subpixel corner/pose fiducial, or a higher-Hz clean-bearing sensor), not a guidance/acquisition/refinement tweak.
  - **Decision.** Subpixel stays DEFAULT-OFF (validated: no robust benefit, honesty-clean, kept as a documented option). The tighter-miss lane is CLOSED with the floor conclusion. Net portfolio value: a rigorous, honest 4-lever negative-results arc converging on the correct root cause (seeker bearing quality + kinematics), which is a stronger engineering story than a lucky tweak. Honesty: paired same-seed, in-project, `gt_*` scoring only.

## ADR-0072 — Builder's three challenges (2026-07-10/11) ADJUDICATED by a 5-agent research workflow: (1) NO sim pivot — STAY on Gazebo; (2) target-realism fix = orient-to-velocity (built, flag-gated) + a VENDORED-mesh demo reskin; (3) the "better tracking" keypoint head is the theoretically-correct upgrade but attacks the WRONG constraint (precision, not the binding acquisition-density / kinematic floor) → optional, pre-registered, likely a 5th null.

- **Context.** Builder pushback (2026-07-10 eve → 07-11): "we got many sub-1 m intercepts on AprilTag, where's this perfect-setup floor claim from? there has to be a better tracking system. The enemy drone is a static, not-even-correctly-oriented model that doesn't resemble a maneuvering drone. Can we pivot to a different sim?" Two of these were partly my error to correct: I had over-generalized the ~1.64 m AprilTag number (which is specifically the **12 m/s WEAVE**, ADR-0055/0056) into a blanket floor — WRONG; sub-meter IS real at slower/straighter regimes (M3 static **0.018–0.040 m**, M4 pursuit **0.945 m**, 9 m/s line/jink **~0.92–1.08 m**). The floor claim is correct ONLY in the hard 12 m/s-weave regime. To answer the three challenges rigorously I ran a background Workflow (`wf_48003243-ca1`, 5 agents): three parallel finders (better-tracking survey, sim-pivot Gazebo-vs-Isaac/AirSim/Flightmare, target-model assessment) → a synthesis → an adversarial review. Verdict: **GO-WITH-CHANGES.**
- **(1) SIM PIVOT — NO. Stay on Gazebo Harmonic + gz-transport + PX4 SITL.** Both complaints that motivate a pivot are **algorithm/modeling issues identical in every simulator**, not Gazebo ceilings: "better than box-center" is a perception-algorithm problem that follows you anywhere, and "static/mis-oriented target" is a MODELING CHOICE already fixable in code. The cost/benefit is decisively negative for a solo builder: a pivot **invalidates every logged number that IS the portfolio** — M3 0.018–0.04 m, M4 0.945 m, the 9/12 m/s miss-vs-speed curves, **Pk@2.5 72/72 (ADR-0064)**, the detection-rate curves, ~70 ADRs — all measured against Gazebo+gz-transport and **non-transferable** to a new renderer/physics; plus it forces a full YOLO seeker **retrain on new pixels** and a rebuild of every world/model (SDF→USD/UE), the mover, mc_batch, and every gate (weeks-to-months). The strongest photoreal candidate (**Isaac Sim 5.1 + Pegasus**) is **below the hardware floor** — needs ≥16 GB GPU + native Linux; this box is a **4070 / 12 GB on WSL2**. The only things a pivot buys (photoreal pixels + banking assets) are reachable far cheaper IN Gazebo: orient-to-velocity (banking) + GPU render (`ogre2`+`GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12`, already queued). And a **non-photoreal renderer with disclosed gaps is MORE defensible** for the sim-to-real story than an Unreal render that tempts over-claiming transfer. (Revisit ONLY if photoreal domain-randomization ever becomes central to the perception thesis AND the builder moves to native Linux + a ≥16 GB GPU — then Pegasus, precisely because it preserves the PX4+MAVSDK interface. Not now.)
- **(2) TARGET REALISM — FIX IN PLACE, two parts.** The builder is RIGHT: `models/fpv_target_markerless/model.sdf` is `<static>true</static>`, all Gazebo PRIMITIVES (no mesh), and `scripts/m4_target_mover.py send_pose()` streamed **position only** (orientation left at identity by ADR-0010 #6, to keep an AprilTag facing the camera — but the markerless model carries NO tag, so that freeze buys nothing here). The target crabbed sideways with a frozen nose, dead-level, never banking/pitching. **Part A — orient-to-velocity (BUILT, this commit):** `velocity_orientation()` + `_euler_zyx_to_quat()` + `--orient-to-velocity` (env `INTERCEPTOR_ORIENT_TO_VEL`) in the mover stream a realistic maneuvering pose — YAW = atan2(vy,vx) to face travel, coordinated-turn BANK into the lateral accel (≤55°, banks toward the turn), nose-down PITCH growing with speed (≤25°) — composed into a normalized quaternion. **Gated OFF by default (byte-identical: `send_pose` leaves the orientation field untouched when `quat is None`, verified 14-byte wire message == pre-fix) and force-REFUSED for `apriltag_target`** (its board must face the camera). `mc_batch` never passes the flag → **every measured MC batch stays byte-identical / unconfounded** (this is the ADR-0010 #6 discipline). 8 unit tests in `tests/test_target_orientation.py` pin the yaw/bank/pitch/identity/norm/model-offset behavior. **Part B — reskin (demo model, PENDING):** swap the primitive silhouette for a real quad mesh **VENDORED into the repo** (copy the BSD-3 `NXP-HGD-CF.dae`, NOT the current symlink into `~/PX4-Autopilot`, which breaks clone-and-run) as a **separate demo model** so the *validated* `fpv_target_markerless` appearance — and thus its NN detection — is NOT changed (a mesh swap is NOT flag-gated; editing the validated model would confound measured detection/Pk). Demo-only + a live seeker-detection check before the video relies on it.
- **(3) BETTER TRACKING — the learned single-keypoint anchor head is the CORRECT design but the WRONG lever here; OPTIONAL + pre-registered.** Survey pick (agreed by synthesis + adversary): a 1-keypoint YOLO-pose head with soft-argmax subpixel decode — the principled successor to ADR-0071's darkness-centroid (a LEARNED semantic point, edge-jitter-immune, ONNX/Hailo-feasible at >14 Hz, honesty-legal camera-pixels-only). REJECTED alternatives: 6DoF PnP pose net (few-pixel target → PnP degenerates; over-engineered for bearing-only) and segmentation-mask centroid (heavy; mask re-quantizes at small scale). **BUT the binding constraint the repo actually names is NOT bearing precision:** at **12 m/s weave** the miss is the ADR-0056 kinematic/bearing-noise FLOOR (even a clean AprilTag = 1.64 m; a precision upgrade cannot go below it — and ADR-0071 just showed the cheap version regressed to a NULL at n=31 because the α-β filter already smooths per-frame jitter); at **slower regimes** the markerless-vs-AprilTag gap is **acquisition DENSITY / recall — missing/late detections (ADR-0038), not imprecise ones.** A keypoint head refines WHERE a box already is; it does not add LOOKS, so it cannot move either binding limit. **Ruling:** do NOT build it speculatively. If built at all, pre-register PAIRED n≥8 against a **RECALL / tail-Pk-robustness** metric (NOT weave median) with an explicit **kill-criterion**, treating it as a likely 5th null. Otherwise **skip it and headline the existing 4-lever negative-results arc** (accel-pred → t_go → tilt → subpixel, all eliminated → "seeker bearing quality + kinematics is the binding floor") — the more mature portfolio story.
- **Adversary's required changes (all adopted):** vendor the mesh into the repo; gate-or-drop the keypoint head against a recall metric with a kill-criterion; GUI sign-check the bank/pitch before shipping the demo; keep `--orient-to-velocity` OFF for every measured batch (logged here so no future reader thinks the banking target confounded a Pk number).
- **Decision / next actions (ordered by portfolio value per effort).** (1) COMMIT the orient-to-velocity fix + tests (this commit — stranded, tested, done). (2) Reskin a demo target with a vendored mesh + red enemy accent. (3) GPU render for the demo. (4) Re-record the demo intercept with the banking mesh target + the ADR-0058 detect-then-track seeker + stereo handoff. (5) OPTIONAL/LOWER — the keypoint head under the pre-registration gate above, else headline the negative-results arc.
- **Honesty.** No `gt_*` in any new path (the target's own velocity/accel drive its own body orientation — the target's motion, not a sensor the interceptor reads); flag-off byte-identical; measured batches unaffected. The 1.64 m over-generalization is corrected on the record above.
- **Date.** 2026-07-11. Research: workflow `wf_48003243-ca1` (5 agents, 351k subagent tokens), verdict GO-WITH-CHANGES.

- **Addendum (2026-07-11) — orient-to-velocity VALIDATED live (seeker + visual).** Two headless sims on the deployed ADR-0058 config confirm the fix works and does NOT break perception. **(1) Intercept flight, markerless world, `INTERCEPTOR_ORIENT_TO_VEL=1`, hero argv (cue-seed 256788, weave 12 m/s):** the banking target was ACQUIRED at **first-dash-detection range 21.62 m**, HANDOFF latched at 9.05 m, and the intercept CLOSED at **miss 0.784 m** (`clean=1 engaged=1 handoff=1`, normal camera-only breakoff; `logs/m4_intercept_pronav_20260711T174723Z.csv`). The mover streamed 395 orientation quaternions with **0 service failures** — gz `set_pose` accepts the added orientation field cleanly. So the maneuvering target detects + intercepts as well as the level one (this single flight even beat the level-target hero capture's 2.076 m, though that is a different seed → not a paired claim). **(2) Chase sign-check, markerless_demo world (stationary `demo_chase_cam`), mover-only weave with orient ON:** side-view frames show the target **banking naturally** — the body rolls into the weave while its ground shadow stays level-X (real roll, not a level slide), presenting changing aspect, nose tracking travel — matching the unit-tested body-axis signs. The `-90°` model-forward offset is correct for `fpv_target_markerless`. **CONCLUSION: `--orient-to-velocity` is validated for the demo** (use for renders; keep OFF for measured MC batches — unchanged). The reskin (Part B) remains optional; the primitive banking target already reads as a red-accented maneuvering quad. Evidence frames: `demo_out/orient_test/{onboard_frames,chase_frames}` (gitignored, regenerable via the scratchpad scripts).

- **Addendum #2 (2026-07-11) — CORRECTION: the "validated banking" read above was WRONG; the target MODEL is a flat billboard, so orientation cannot make it look like a real quad. Model replacement + seeker retrain now REQUIRED (builder-directed).** The builder viewed a clearer frame and correctly diagnosed it: the target's rotor plane is VERTICAL and "moving only tilts this vertical drone plane." Re-reading `models/fpv_target_markerless/model.sdf` confirms it: the 4 arms sit at local `(0.14, ±0.205, ±0.205)` and the props at `(0.17, ±0.354, ±0.354)` — i.e. the whole quad is laid out in the local **Y-Z plane (VERTICAL, Z is up)**, a flat BILLBOARD facing -X (the SDF header: "yaw=-90deg swaps the box's long axis onto local Y"; inherited from `fpv_target`'s tag-panel-facing-the-camera design). It reads as a quad ONLY head-on from the approaching onboard camera; from any other angle — or once oriented — it is a tilting vertical wall. My chase-frame "natural banking" read was a MISREAD of this billboard rotating (honest error, corrected here). **The `velocity_orientation()` MATH is fine** (it applies a correct world quaternion; unit tests stand); the defect is the MODEL geometry. **Fix (builder-directed, retrain accepted):** a proper 3D quad target with a HORIZONTAL rotor plane — using the real x500 airframe mesh, red-tinted enemy (`models/fpv_quad_enemy/`, new, leaves the validated model intact). This changes the camera's view of the target (edge-on/oblique instead of a face-on billboard — MORE realistic), so the markerless seeker (`drone_finetuned_v2`, fine-tuned on the billboard renders, `sim_dataset_v2`) must be RETRAINED (v3) and the intercept RE-VALIDATED (Pk/miss). Plan: build model → render-verify the banking looks real → capture v3 dataset → fine-tune → re-validate → swap in. Supersedes Addendum #1's "validated for the demo" claim for the markerless target.

- **Addendum #3 (2026-07-11) — quad-target arc EXECUTED: model DONE + verified, level seeker re-validated (single flight, promising-but-late-handoff), banking-pose retrain IN FLIGHT. Full resume guide: `docs/quad_target_retrain.md`.** Status is deliberate — read the verbs.
  - **Model `fpv_quad_enemy` — DONE + render-verified (`53b0400`).** A proper 3D quad: real x500 airframe mesh (`NXP-HGD-CF.dae`) + 5010 motor bells + 1345 props, **HORIZONTAL rotor plane, nose baked +X** (so `--orient-yaw-offset-deg 0`, unlike the billboard's `-90`), red enemy accents. Meshes **VENDORED into the repo** (~25 MB — a git-LFS candidate once a remote exists; ADR-0072 required vendoring, no symlink into `~/PX4-Autopilot`). Chase render (`worlds/quad_enemy_verify.sdf`, mover `--orient-to-velocity --orient-yaw-offset-deg 0`, 3184 poses, 0 gz set_pose failures) CONFIRMS it banks like a real drone (body rolls into the weave, rotor disk stays horizontal) — the billboard defect is fixed.
  - **Retrain pipeline — DONE (`0d9d20e`).** `worlds/quad_enemy.sdf` (= markerless.sdf with the target swapped to `fpv_quad_enemy`, onboard cam only, symlinked into PX4). `scripts/seeker/render_sim_dataset.py` captured a gt-labeled level+yaw grid (1320 imgs, gitignored/regenerable, `scripts/seeker/data/quad_dataset`). `scripts/seeker/train_daemon_quad.py` (setsid-detached yolo11n, imgsz 640, 60 ep, sentinel `QUAD_TRAIN_EXPORT_DONE`) → `scripts/seeker/weights/drone_finetuned_quad.{pt,onnx}`. HONESTY unchanged: gt auto-labels TRAINING data offline only (orientation-invariant bounding-sphere box); no gt reaches inference.
  - **Level seeker RE-VALIDATED — ONE flight, NOT a Pk claim.** On `quad_enemy` the retrained level seeker **first-detects at 23.3 m** (beats v2's 21.6 m) and **intercepts at 1.18 m clean** — **BUT handoff is LATE at 1.91 m** (v2 hands off ~9 m) with **26 tracker re-acquisitions**: detection is INTERMITTENT on the BANKED aspects the level+yaw dataset never showed. Log `logs/m4_intercept_pronav_20260711T195539Z.csv`. Core works; early/stable handoff is the open gap. **No Pk batch has been run on the new pair.**
  - **Banking-pose retrain — IN FLIGHT (scripts on disk, UNCOMMITTED).** `render_sim_dataset_banked.py` (teleport extended to roll+pitch+yaw; banks to ±50°, pitch to +20°; gt box orientation-invariant so labels stay valid) → banked frames in `scripts/seeker/data/quad_dataset_banked` → `merge_quad_v2.py` unions level+banked into `quad_dataset_v2` → `train_daemon_quad_v2.py` (setsid, sentinel `QUAD_V2_TRAIN_EXPORT_DONE`) → `drone_finetuned_quad_v2.{pt,onnx}`. **As of last check only the banked CAPTURE is running** (~780/198 frames written); the merge and v2-train launch have NOT happened yet. Resume/check/export/re-validate/swap commands are in `docs/quad_target_retrain.md` (the scratchpad repro scripts are ephemeral — the durable commands live there).
  - **Swap NOT done; fallback stays live.** `fpv_target_markerless` + `drone_finetuned_v2` remain the deployed pair and the rollback. Swap only AFTER a Pk batch validates the new pair — never before. Every prior Pk/detection/miss number was measured against the billboard model and is unaffected by this arc until a deliberate, Pk-gated swap.

## ADR-0073 — "BANK-AS-ACCELERATION CUE" for maneuver-predictive guidance (builder idea, 2026-07-11): read the target's BANK ANGLE from the seeker to estimate its lateral acceleration DIRECTLY (tan φ = a_lat/g) — a LEADING maneuver indicator that BYPASSES the position-differentiation noise wall which killed ADR-0069. Newly enabled by the proper 3D banking quad (`fpv_quad_enemy`). FEASIBILITY-GATED on bank-estimation accuracy from a few-pixel target.

- **Context.** Builder (2026-07-11), seeing the new banking quad, asked: can we "detect how the drone is going to move based on the way it's banking" for a better maneuvering-intercept threshold, if it fits the specced Pi+Hailo hardware? This is a NEW idea the `fpv_quad_enemy` model UNLOCKS — the old billboard target didn't bank, so there was no bank cue to read (ADR-0072 add. #2).
- **The physics (why it's sound).** A coordinated turn satisfies `tan(bank) = a_lat / g`, so the **bank angle IS the lateral acceleration** — obtained WITHOUT differentiating anything. And bank is a **LEADING indicator**: roll → lift-vector tilt → lateral accel → velocity rotates, so the bank precedes the path curvature by the roll+integration lag (~0.1–0.3 s for a small quad). Reading it lets an APN/accel term start nulling the maneuver BEFORE the LOS rate develops.
- **Why it RE-OPENS ADR-0069 (which REJECTED accel-prediction).** ADR-0069's kill-gate (`scripts/experiments/dash_accel_lead_probe.py`) rejected accel-prediction because the ONLY accel source was double-differentiating the noisy monocular position track: noise **7.07** vs signal **4.71 m/s²**, SNR < 1 — a hard noise wall. **Bank-based accel needs NO differentiation**: its noise ≈ `g·σ_φ/cos²φ`. For a bank-estimate σ_φ ≤ ~15° the accel noise is ~1–2.5 m/s² < the 4.71 signal → **SNR > 1**, flipping the exact reason ADR-0069 failed. So this is a fundamentally cleaner accel source, not a re-proposal of the rejected mechanism.
- **Hardware + honesty.** A small **bank-angle regression head** on the existing YOLO detector (or a YOLO-pose-style scalar output) — runs on the same Pi+Hailo NPU class the seeker is specced for (>14 Hz, ADR-0063), a minor addition not a new model. Honesty-legal: the bank is ESTIMATED FROM PIXELS, never `gt_*`; feeds guidance exactly like the bearing does.
- **Honest payoff bound (ADR-0023/0069).** The miss is ~84% a kinematic floor set at handoff; the maneuver term is only ~0.27 m of the ~1.6 m 12 m/s-weave miss → **small gain on the CURRENT mild weave**. The value SCALES with maneuver AGGRESSIVENESS (harder jinks/turns = bigger maneuver term) — the "maneuvering intercept threshold" the builder is after. Also capacity-bounded by ½·a·t_go² (t_go shrinks as you close), and there is a real accuracy-vs-range-vs-t_go tension (bank is easiest to read up close, where t_go is smallest).
- **THE FEASIBILITY GATE (design-time, before any guidance sim — same discipline as ADR-0069).** Can a learned head estimate bank to ~±10–15° from the target at handoff range (~10 m ≈ 46 px span, and out to mid-course ~20 m ≈ 23 px)? **The `quad_dataset_banked` capture (1344 frames at KNOWN banks ±30/±50° across ranges 2–8 m) is exactly the test set** — train a bank-regression head on it, measure bank-error vs range, and check whether the implied accel-SNR clears the ADR-0069 wall. PASS → lab APN-with-bank-accel A/B vs plain pro-nav on AGGRESSIVE maneuvers → Gazebo decides (lab ranks, Gazebo decides). FAIL (bank too noisy at range) → honest NULL, logged like ADR-0069.
- **Decision.** IDEA CAPTURED + assessed as promising and worth the feasibility gate. GATED behind the current banking retrain (sim busy). **Next:** after the `drone_finetuned_quad_v2` re-validation, run the bank-estimation feasibility on `quad_dataset_banked` (design-time, no new sim). If it clears the SNR wall, council the guidance mechanization (APN vs a small Kalman-CA fed by the bank-accel) before building. Do NOT build the guidance term before the perception gate passes.
- **Date.** 2026-07-11.

## ADR-0074 — FOVEATED / AUTO-CROP detection + higher-res sensor (builder idea, 2026-07-11): detect on a downscaled whole frame, then crop the FULL-RES ROI (+margin) and re-run the SAME detector on just the target — high-res detail on the drone at ~2× a cheap pass, not a full-sensor high-res pass. We ALREADY downscale 1280→640, so auto-crop recovers pixels-on-target FOR FREE (no new camera); a higher-res sensor extends it. Attacks detection ROBUSTNESS/RANGE (ADR-0038) + is a near-prerequisite for the bank cue (ADR-0073). Disclosure + re-earn-M1/M2 + σ_R-rescale gated (ADR-0025 honesty).

- **Context.** Builder (2026-07-11): if higher resolution helps, use a higher-res camera — but save compute with an **auto-crop**: find the drone on downscaled low-res frame(s), then crop into that region (+ a bit) on the full-res feed and run the same model on just the drone (lower res than the whole sensor, but only on the target). Correct instinct + a standard technique.
- **What it is (named).** Coarse-to-fine / foveated / ROI-cascade / "detect-then-zoom" / digital-PTZ detection (tiling cousin = SAHI). Standard for small-object detection on edge hardware. Composes with the ADR-0058 detect-then-track loop, which ALREADY crops to the tracked box for CSRT.
- **THE FREE WIN — we already discard resolution.** The camera is **1280×960** but `finetuned_seeker.py` runs the detector at **imgsz 640** → every frame is downscaled before inference, so a ~46 px native target is ~23 px to the net. Cropping the NATIVE 1280×960 around the target and running the detector on that crop hands the net ~2× the pixels-on-target **with no new camera**. A physically higher-res sensor extends the same trick.
- **Where it helps HERE (honest).** (1) **Detection ROBUSTNESS/RANGE — timely:** the current v2 late-handoff / intermittent-banked-detection is a detection-DENSITY problem (ADR-0038); more pixels-on-target is a direct lever → steadier lock through the bank. (2) **Bank cue (ADR-0073) — natural pairing / near-prerequisite:** estimating bank off a ~23 px blob is hard; off a ~90 px native crop it's plausible — auto-crop likely gates whether the bank-estimation feasibility passes. (3) **The mild-weave MISS — probably NOT:** kinematic/filter-floored (why ADR-0071 subpixel was null); resolution buys detection quality (earlier/steadier handoff + the bank cue), not a smaller CPA on the easy target.
- **THE HONESTY TRIPWIRE (ADR-0025).** ADR-0025's council CUT "camera FOV/resolution WIDENING" — but specifically as **tag-inflation** (making the fiducial bigger on the sensor = secretly easier, laundered as algorithm). A **higher-res sensor for genuine small-target detection is DIFFERENT and legitimate** (ADR-0038 already named "higher train imgsz for smaller-target range"): allowed IF disclosed, M1/M2 re-earned, and σ_R (∝ 1/f_px, ADR-0029 line 1235) rescaled — NEVER to quietly inflate a Pk. Auto-crop-into-native-res mostly sidesteps even this (not changing the sensor, just not discarding captured pixels).
- **Sim caveat.** A higher-res CAMERA in Gazebo costs RENDER time (full-res high-rate consumption tanked RTF, ADR-0029 probe line 1242) — that's the queued GPU-render switch's job. The auto-crop COMPUTE saving lands on the real Pi+Hailo, not in the sim.
- **Decision.** IDEA CAPTURED + assessed feasible/legitimate. **Cheapest first step (near-free, no new camera): an auto-crop stage that runs the detector on the NATIVE-res crop instead of the 640 downscale** — test whether it steadies the v2 banked detection (earlier handoff) and improves range. Then, if a physically higher-res sensor is added, disclose + re-earn M1/M2 + rescale σ_R. Sequence: pairs with the ADR-0073 bank-estimation gate (both want pixels-on-target) — do the auto-crop experiment alongside/before the bank-head feasibility. Council the sensor-spec change if a real higher-res camera is adopted (touches ADR-0012/0025).
- **Date.** 2026-07-11.

- **Addendum (2026-07-11) — "why not just a 4K camera?" (builder). YES — and it makes the auto-crop the ENABLER, not an alternative.** 4K sensors are cheap/abundant; camera cost was never the constraint. The constraint is COMPUTE/BANDWIDTH: (1) the detector ingests a fixed 640 px input, so a full 4K frame is DOWNSCALED to 640 before inference — throwing the 4K detail away (you'd pay 8.3 MP and hand the net the same 640 it sees now). **The ONLY ways to USE 4K are (a) auto-crop the native-4K ROI → run the detector on the crop (target 23 px → ~90+ px at low compute), or (b) SAHI-tile — both are this ADR's foveation.** Full-frame 4K inference on a Pi+Hailo at frame rate is the part that does NOT fit; cropped 4K fits. So 4K + auto-crop are ONE system. **Spec caveats where "cheap" bites:** (i) **GLOBAL shutter, not rolling** — cheap 4K is rolling-shutter CMOS; a fast BANKING target gets line-by-line skew that directly corrupts a bank-angle estimate (ADR-0073's signal); the sim assumes ZERO rolling shutter (disclosed upper bound, ADR-0007/0010) so a rolling-shutter 4K underperforms the sim → spec global shutter OR model the skew (worse-than-ideal mandate). (ii) **Windowed/ROI readout** at high fps = the hardware version of the auto-crop (high-fps zoomed feed on the target). (iii) **Sim render cost:** 4K in Gazebo tanks RTF (ADR-0029) → validate the auto-crop BENEFIT at moderate res + GPU render, not literal 4K render (the effect = pixels-on-target, which scales). (iv) honesty: re-earn M1/M2 + rescale σ_R on any res change. **Net: plan a 4K (ideally global-shutter) sensor; the auto-crop is the mechanism that converts its pixels into cheap high-density looks — enabling the bank cue (ADR-0073), steadier tracking, and acquisition range.**

- **Addendum #2 (2026-07-11) — "why can't the sim do 4K, and why not just a high shutter speed?" (builder). Two clarifications.** **(1) 4K in the sim is RENDERER-limited, not application-limited.** Gazebo accepts any camera resolution (`<width>/<height>`); there is NO app block. The blocker is that we render on the **CPU (llvmpipe software rasterizer)** in WSL2 with the RTX 4070 IDLE — 8.3 MP/frame in software crawls, and ADR-0029 MEASURED that even today's res, consumed at full-rate, collapses RTF to ~0.04 (breaks PX4 arming/offboard/physics timing). **Fix = the queued GPU-render switch (ogre2 + d3d12):** a 4070 renders 4K@60 in games, so a single 4K camera is trivial for it — caveats: the d3d12 path is WSL-flaky + can disturb Windows GPU apps (why it's not on yet), and 4K TRANSPORT+consume is still heavy (→ the auto-crop). So: not with CPU-software render, YES with GPU render — a config change, not a wall. EMPIRICALLY TESTABLE: render a 4K cam, log RTF with/without GPU render (run when the sim is free from training). **(2) High shutter speed does NOT fix rolling shutter — different mechanisms.** Shutter speed = exposure time → fixes motion BLUR. Rolling shutter = line-by-line READOUT over a readout-time → a fast crosser is at different positions on different rows → geometric SKEW ("jello"), which depends on READOUT time, not exposure. A fast shutter freezes each row but the rows still read at staggered times → skew survives. Fixes for skew: **global shutter** (zero skew, clean spec), fast-readout sensor (small skew), or software de-skew. Quantitatively the skew is SMALL at handoff (~a few px, ~1–3% of target width; grows toward CPA as LOS rate spikes) — tolerable for detection/tracking, a real error source for precise BANK estimation (ADR-0073) → global shutter is the safe spec. Note: fast shutter needs LIGHT (short exposure starves the sensor at dawn/dusk — ties to the day-vs-all-conditions sensor tradeoff, ADR-0025/parent).

## ADR-0075 — GPU RENDER ADOPTED for Gazebo camera sensors (builder-directed, 2026-07-11): `GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12` routes rendering to the RTX 4070 via the WSL2 D3D12 path instead of the llvmpipe software rasterizer. MEASURED RTF **0.336 → 0.951** (2.8×) with a consumed camera; images VISUALLY EQUIVALENT. Wired into every sim-boot script via a sourced, toggleable snippet.

- **Context.** Builder (2026-07-11): "are we on GPU for the sims yet? if not implement that now." We were NOT — Gazebo rendered on the CPU (llvmpipe software OpenGL) in WSL2 with the 4070 idle (headless-sim-is-cpu-bound memory; ADR-0029 measured that a consumed full-res camera collapses RTF). The GPU-render switch was queued; this implements it.
- **Measured A/B** (`quad_enemy`, onboard camera consumed, RTF = manifest sim_t-span / wall-span): **llvmpipe RTF 0.336, GPU util ~3% (idle), 173 MiB, 154 frames/15 s** vs **d3d12 RTF 0.951, GPU util 12–31% (rendering), 451 MiB, 437 frames/15 s (full 30 Hz).** ~2.8× faster, near real-time, no errors. Sourced-export path (scripts/sim_gpu_render.sh) gives the same result (RTF 0.907) as inline env.
- **Mechanism.** `GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12` + `MESA_D3D12_DEFAULT_ADAPTER_NAME=NVIDIA` make Mesa render OpenGL through the WSL2 D3D12 backend (`/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/dri/d3d12_dri.so`, `/usr/lib/wsl/lib/libd3d12*.so`) → the RTX 4070. OGRE2 camera sensors render offscreen on the GPU even headless.
- **Implementation.** `scripts/sim_gpu_render.sh` EXPORTS the env (inherited by the `env … HEADLESS=1 make px4_sitl gz_x500_mono_cam` boot — env(1) adds, never resets). Sourced (one line, idempotent, path-robust) at the top of all 17 sim-boot `.sh` scripts (mc_batch, check_m0–m4/s1/s2/t16/t19, probe_stereo_rtf, run_multirange_capture, run_t17v2, seeker/{capture_pass,capture_pass_v3,grid_capture_v3,probe_v2}). **Toggle `SIM_GPU_RENDER=0` forces software** (the escape hatch if d3d12 flakes); auto-falls-back if the d3d12 driver is absent.
- **Equivalence / honesty (ADR-0010 discipline).** GPU vs software render is the SAME OGRE2 scene + shaders, different rasterizer — a static-scene CPU-vs-GPU frame pair is **VISUALLY IDENTICAL** (target, lighting, shadow, horizon; no artifacts), so the detector sees the same pixels → detection/Pk unchanged. **NUMERICALLY CONFIRMED (ADR-0076): a paired same-seed CPU-vs-GPU comparison gave equivalent first-det (14.7 vs 15.2 m) and miss — the equivalence holds, not just visually.** (The upcoming quad Pk batch was GPU-rendered; the historical billboard 72/72 (ADR-0064) was software-rendered but is not re-run (that baseline is retired with the billboard target anyway).
- **Caveats.** d3d12 on WSLg is known-flaky and can disturb other Windows GPU apps (the `SIM_GPU_RENDER=0` toggle is the fallback). The 4070 has 8 GB — fine for the current cameras; watch VRAM if 4K + multiple cameras (ADR-0074) run at once.
- **Decision.** GPU render is the DEFAULT for all sims (2.8× faster batches/captures/demo, and it UNBLOCKS 4K/higher-res per ADR-0074). Reversible per-run via `SIM_GPU_RENDER=0`. Also a candidate global stability win (ADR-0029 saw d3d12 make the no-rig control rock-solid).
- **Date.** 2026-07-11.

## ADR-0076 — QUAD Pk on the STANDARD batch geometry is POOR (~2/45 Pk@2.5) vs the billboard's 72/72 — the realistic 3D quad is detected INTERMITTENTLY (late handoff → high miss); the flat billboard FLATTERED the project's headline detection. GPU render is NOT the cause (paired CPU/GPU confirmed equivalent — ADR-0075 vindicated).

- **Context.** First real Pk batch of the quad (`fpv_quad_enemy` + `drone_finetuned_quad_v2` seeker, orient ON, weave 12 m/s, standard mc_batch geometry, master-seed 42): **Pk@2.5 ≈ 2/45, miss median ~3.4 m, misses clustered 2.5–4 m and 6.5–7.7 m.** Wildly worse than the earlier "characterization 6/6 clean."
- **First hypothesis (GPU render domain-shift) — REFUTED.** The seeker trained on CPU(llvmpipe)-rendered images; I'd just switched to GPU(d3d12) render, so I suspected a domain shift degrading small-target detection. **Paired same-seed comparison (12 flights, CPU vs GPU render):** first-det median **CPU 14.7 m vs GPU 15.2 m** (equivalent), miss median CPU 4.94 vs GPU 3.06 (both bad), Pk@2.5 CPU 0/12 vs GPU 2/12. **→ render is NOT the culprit; ADR-0075's CPU≈GPU equivalence is CONFIRMED, not just visual.** (My earlier "GPU might be degrading detection" worry was wrong — logged honestly.)
- **The REAL cause — geometry exposed it, and it's an honesty finding.** My "characterization 6/6 clean" used a NON-STANDARD easy geometry (target 30 m out → long dash → lots of lock time). The STANDARD batch geometry (target ~14 m out → fast, short crossing — the EXACT geometry the billboard scored **72/72** on, ADR-0064) gives the quad only ~14–15 m first-det and **late handoff ~1.5 m** → high miss. On the SAME geometry: **billboard 72/72, quad ~2/45.** So the flat, always-face-on billboard was EASY-MODE for the detector and **flattered the project's headline Pk**; a real 3D quad seen edge-on + banking during a fast crossing is detected INTERMITTENTLY inside the handoff gate → the handoff latches too late → the terminal has no correction time.
- **NOT yet concluded fundamental vs fixable.** The seeker DOES acquire at ~15 m then loses lock intermittently inside 10 m; the few flights that latched an early handoff (8–10 m) hit **0.66–0.78 m**. That "acquires but can't hold the 2-consecutive streak" signature may be part the harder target AND part the CSRT/re-acquisition gate (`seed_gate_m=8.0`, `reseed_iou=0.2`) tuned for the RIGID billboard, not a hard-jinking quad (cf. the 43 rejected re-acqs in the single re-validation). Next: investigate the late-handoff mechanism (detection-density vs gate-rejection) before calling it fundamental.
- **Decision.** (1) **NO SWAP** — the quad Pk is not validated (it's poor); billboard + `drone_finetuned_v2` stay the baseline (now known to be detection-flattered). (2) Correct ADR-0075: GPU render IS equivalent (paired-confirmed) — keep it for speed. (3) The quad detection-consistency bottleneck is the new target; it directly motivates the bank cue (ADR-0073) + 4K/auto-crop (ADR-0074) — more pixels-on-target + earlier acquisition. (4) Investigate the tracker/re-acq gate for the maneuvering quad (possible partial fix). (5) HONEST framing for the portfolio: the realistic target reveals the billboard flattered detection — a stronger integrity story than a flattering number.
- **Date.** 2026-07-12.

- **Addendum (2026-07-12) — the bottleneck is the CSRT TRACKER, not NN blindness → more FIXABLE than "fundamentally too hard".** Detect-track stats across the batch flights: **`reacq_rejected ≈ 0`** (the re-acq gate is NOT the problem — corrects the single-flight "43 rejected" worry), NN coverage **~50%** (nn_frames ~470/flight — the NN DOES see the quad about half the time), but **`lost ≈ seeds ≈ 20–27` per flight** — the CSRT tracker acquires→loses→re-seeds ~25× per flight. Mechanism: CSRT is a correlation/appearance tracker; the RIGID billboard held lock (72/72), but a BANKING quad changes appearance frame-to-frame so CSRT keeps losing it → flickering lock → the 2-consecutive in-range streak for handoff rarely forms until ~1.5 m → late handoff → high miss. So the quad's poor Pk is LARGELY a tracker-holding failure on a maneuvering target (partly tunable) layered on a genuinely harder-to-detect target (NN ~50% vs the billboard's near-100%). **Cheap next levers (now fast on GPU render):** (1) NN-only seeker (drop CSRT; the NN's ~50% + the α-β filter may beat a flickering tracker) or higher NN re-validate cadence (every 2–3 frames vs 8) — less reliance on CSRT; (2) a tracker robust to appearance change; (3) the fundamental detection lift — bank cue (ADR-0073) + 4K/auto-crop (ADR-0074, more pixels → higher NN coverage). Test the cheap tracker levers FIRST (a flag flip + a small batch) before concluding anything is fundamental.

- **Addendum #2 (2026-07-12) — TRACKER A/B DONE (fixes #1/#2): NN-ONLY WINS; every tracker (CSRT + learned VIT) HURTS the maneuvering quad. The ~50% NN-coverage ceiling remains → fix #3 is the real lever.** Paired n=8, standard geometry, quad + v2 seeker, GPU render: **Pk@2.5 = CSRT-8 1/8, NN-only 4/8, revalidate-2 0/8, revalidate-4 0/8, VIT 0/8.** NN-only (drop `--track`) quadruples Pk@2.5 vs CSRT and beats the learned ViT tracker. **Interpretation:** ADR-0058 detect-then-track was built for the RIGID billboard (a tracker holds a stable lock + densifies between NN frames); a hard-maneuvering quad slips ANY tracker's lock (CSRT flickers ~25×/flight; VIT also fails), so the tracked bearing is WORSE than the NN's own detections → NN-only is best. More re-validation didn't help (0/8). **Decision:** the quad's deployment config drops `--track` (NN-only); VIT stays a committed option (didn't help here, may help a less-aggressive target). BUT ~50% Pk = the NN's ~50% detection COVERAGE ceiling — no tracker can exceed it. **The only path past ~50% is raising NN coverage on the edge-on/banking quad = fix #3: 4K/auto-crop + higher-imgsz retrain (ADR-0074, more pixels-on-target) and the bank cue (ADR-0073, guidance).** Coverage-first (auto-crop / imgsz) is the direct Pk lever; the bank cue helps the maneuver term on top.

- **Addendum #3 (2026-07-12) — fix #3 resolution PROBE (cheap, no-retrain) + the 3-fix PROGRAM outcome.** Re-exported the v2 seeker at imgsz 1280 (native full-res of the 1280 frame, no downscale) and ran NN-only paired vs the 640 arm: **Pk@2.5 640 4/8 vs 1280 2/8** (within n=8 noise), but **handoff 1.62 → 3.87 m** (native res DOES improve detection consistency/range) with slightly WORSE miss — the classic scale-mismatch signature (a 640-TRAINED model fed 1280 detects earlier but noisier/more phantoms). **Conclusion: resolution is a real coverage lever, but NOT free — it requires RETRAINING at the higher resolution / on native crops, not a re-export.** **3-FIX PROGRAM OUTCOME (builder "implement all 3"):** **#1 tracker levers — DONE: NN-only wins** (drop `--track` for the maneuvering quad; CSRT/VIT both hurt). **#2 appearance-robust tracker — DONE: VIT integrated but does NOT help** (shelved as an option; every tracker slips the banking lock). **#3 fundamental lifts — CHARACTERIZED, build pending:** the binding ceiling is NN detection COVERAGE (~50%) on the edge-on/banking quad; the cheap resolution probe confirms resolution helps detection but needs a retrain. The remaining fix-#3 BUILDS (each a real arc): (a) **higher-res / native-crop RETRAIN** (imgsz 1280 retrain ≈ ~7 h CPU, OR the compute-efficient AUTO-CROP = a native-crop dataset + a normal-cost 640 retrain on crops — ADR-0074, the better path); (b) the **bank cue** (ADR-0073, guidance lever, on top of coverage). Net honest position: the realistic quad's Pk on the standard geometry is ~50% (vs the billboard's flattering 72/72); the tracker fix recovered the tracker-induced losses; a coverage-raising RETRAIN (auto-crop) is the real path to a high Pk. **NO swap; billboard baseline stands.**

- **Addendum #4 (2026-07-12, overnight) — HONEST QUAD BASELINE (NN-only 640, n=32): Pk@2.5 = 13/32 = 41% (Wilson-95 LB 26%), Pk@2.8 44%, miss median 4.63 m, bimodal (min 0.22 clean / max 8.11 complete-miss), handoff median 2.46 m.** This is the realistic maneuvering quad's Pk with the best current config (drop `--track`, v2 seeker, orient ON, standard geometry) — vs the billboard's ~99% (72/72). The ~41% quantifies the honesty finding solidly at n=32: the flat billboard flattered detection by ~2.4×. The bimodal miss = ~41% clean intercepts vs ~59% complete misses (target flies past when the ~50%-coverage NN loses it). The fix-#3 AUTO-CROP coverage retrain (running overnight) targets exactly this: raise NN coverage → fewer complete misses → higher Pk. Success bar for the auto-crop: beat 41% Pk@2.5 (NN-only + `MARKERLESS_AUTOCROP=1` + crop-trained weights, same n=32/seeds).

- **Addendum #5 (2026-07-12, overnight) — THE 41% IS A DIRECTIONAL FAILURE, likely PHANTOMS in r2l — and the auto-crop should FIX it.** Baseline n=32 split by direction: **l2r Pk@2.5 = 13/16 = 81% (miss med 1.16 m); r2l = 0/16 = 0% (miss med 7.43 m).** So the quad+seeker intercepts GREAT one way and TOTALLY fails the other — NOT a uniform ~50% ceiling. r2l is NOT a coverage failure: it detects fine (first-det ~15 m, coverage ~57%) and hands off (~1.6 m median), but then misses ~7 m with **`handoff_cue_rejects=19`** (camera bearing disagrees with the cue by >8 m). An 8 m error at 15 m range is far larger than any box-center wobble (a real-target box is off by ≤ the ~1 m target size) → it is a **PHANTOM** — the NN locks a false high-confidence target off-target in the r2l aspect's background (ADR-0057 phantom class), guidance chases it → 7 m miss. **Why this is hopeful: the AUTO-CROP (crop to the cue/last-detection ROI) EXCLUDES the phantom-triggering background** → should kill the r2l phantoms and recover close to l2r's 81%. The overnight crop retrain + `MARKERLESS_AUTOCROP=1` validation tests exactly this. If the auto-crop fixes r2l, the quad jumps from 41% toward ~80% (l2r-level) — a real close-interception win, and a cleaner story than "the quad is hard" (it's a fixable per-aspect phantom). If it does NOT, next is a phantom-hardening pass on the r2l aspect (negative-mining the r2l background, or the cue-gated crop as the phantom filter). NOTE: also re-examine the tally's `first_det` column (it printed impossible <handoff values — a column bug, not a data bug; the flight-log first-dets like 15.11 m are correct).

- **Addendum #6 (2026-07-12) — r2l PHANTOM CONFIRMED from the flight CSV.** In a r2l flight (`m4_intercept_pronav_20260712T100321Z.csv`): camera-vs-gt target-position error median **3.97 m, max 22.9 m**, with **43/78 detected ticks >3 m off truth**; the phantom reports `meas_range ≈ 1.5 m` while the TRUE range is 15–20 m (a big false box reads as "close"). So r2l is a hard NN false-positive problem, not coverage/geometry. **Fix priority (overnight): (1) the auto-crop validation — the crop-trained NN runs on a TARGET-CENTERED crop that excludes the phantom-triggering background, so it should not fire the phantom (the direct test, running). RISK: the auto-crop bootstraps off the last detection / full-frame fallback, and the crop-trained NN on a FULL frame is a scale mismatch — if acquisition/handoff suffers, the fix is a two-model coarse-to-fine (v2 full-frame to ACQUIRE + crop-NN to refine) or cue-gated crop centering. (2) If the auto-crop underperforms: a RANGE-PLAUSIBILITY gate (reject a camera detection whose implied range disagrees grossly with the cue pre-handoff / the extrapolated last-good range post-handoff — kills the "1.5 m" phantom directly), and/or negative-mine the r2l background. l2r (81%) shows the guidance + geometry are sound — this is purely r2l perception false-positives.** These are the concrete next levers if the auto-crop doesn't fully recover r2l.

- **Addendum #7 (2026-07-12, overnight) — THE r2l FIX IS `--track` (a FREE config flip, NOT negative-mining or a retrain): the CSRT tracker's temporal consistency FILTERS the phantom that NN-only chases.** Testing the r2l phantom with the DEPLOYED detect-then-track config on the failing geometry: 6 flights (quad_v2_batch geometry — target 6.5,30 crossing −y @12 m/s + weave, `--track --handoff-cue-gate 8`, v2 seeker, orient ON, GPU render) → **r2l Pk@2.5 = 5/6 (83%), miss = 0.80/1.22/0.90/0.92/1.55/2.83 m** — vs NN-only's **0/16 (0%)**. Mechanism: the phantom is a TRANSIENT high-confidence false box; **NN-only hands off on the first confident detection (often the phantom → chases it → ~7 m miss)**, but **`--track` requires a 2-consecutive in-range streak and CSRT's frame-to-frame correlation rejects the flickering phantom → it locks the REAL target → clean intercept**. (Confirmed ironic: the capture flights hit 5/6 r2l *despite* 682 phantom-firing frames present in those same flights — the tracker filtered them live.) **This REVISES add #2's "NN-only wins, --track hurts":** that verdict was AGGREGATE on one geometry, not direction-split. The DIRECTION-SPLIT truth: NN-only wins l2r (81%, the phantom-free aspect where CSRT flicker on the fast closer only hurts), `--track` wins r2l (the phantom aspect where CSRT's consistency is decisive). **Confirming on the EXACT baseline geometry** (mc_batch n=32 master-seed 42 + `--track`, direction-split vs the NN-only baseline `mc_batch_20260712T093419Z.csv`) — RUNNING (`mc_quad_track_ab_*`). **The config decision hinges on --track's l2r number:** if `--track` holds l2r ≳ NN-only → adopt `--track` globally (simplest); if `--track` hurts l2r → **DIRECTION-AWARE config** (the S2 cue KNOWS the crossing direction from its velocity vector, legally pre-handoff → the seeker picks NN-only for l2r / `--track` for r2l — still free, no retrain). Either way this takes the quad from 41% toward ~80%+ in BOTH directions = the close-interception win, cheaper and cleaner than a coverage retrain. Negative-mining is now the FALLBACK (682 phantom frames mined → `scripts/seeker/data/quad_dataset_hardened` (565 hard-negs); retrain PAUSED/preserved — resume only if the tracker config underperforms).

- **⚠️ Addendum #8 (2026-07-12, overnight) — add #7 CORRECTED/RETRACTED: `--track` is NOT the fix — my capture geometry was NON-REPRESENTATIVE (easier).** The A/B (mc_batch n=32 master-seed 42 + `--track`, the CANONICAL geometry) contradicts the capture: **`--track` l2r = 0/5 (0%, ~3 m miss, clean but outside 2.5) vs baseline NN-only l2r 81%; `--track` r2l = 0/4 (still ~7 m phantom)** — 0/9 partial, WORSE than NN-only both directions. **Root cause of my error:** the capture used quad_v2_batch's geometry (target start **~30 m** away, `--target-start=6.5,30`), but the canonical baseline mc_batch starts the target **~13.6 m** away (master-seed 42) — a much shorter/compressed engagement. `--track`'s r2l 5/6 win holds ONLY at the easier 30 m start; on the canonical 13.6 m geometry the CSRT tracker flickers (`lost=5/12/22`/flight) and DEGRADES the clean NN l2r bearing from ~1.2 m to ~3 m miss (this CONFIRMS add #2's "--track hurts") while still failing r2l. **Lesson (methodology):** reproduce the CANONICAL gate geometry (master-seed 42), not a convenient fixed start — a farther start is a materially easier engagement. **The r2l fix reverts to NEGATIVE-MINING** (the 682 mined phantoms + `quad_dataset_hardened` retrain — RESUME it), with the caveat that the mined phantoms came from the 30 m capture too, so **validate the hardened NN on the CANONICAL baseline r2l geometry**; if the 30 m phantoms don't transfer, re-mine from master-seed-42 r2l flights. NN-only (drop `--track`) remains the deployed config (81% l2r); the honest quad number stays 41% until a validated coverage/phantom fix lands.

- **⚠️ Addendum #9 (2026-07-12, overnight) — INDEPENDENT AUDIT (Opus, sim-free) corrects the r2l MECHANISM and flags the mining as possibly NULL. The 41% baseline is SOLID; the FIX premise was not.** A skeptical methodology review of the whole overnight arc found:
  - **The phantom is UNIVERSAL, not r2l-specific → add #5's "l2r = the phantom-free aspect" is WRONG.** The ~1.5 m phantom (`min_meas_range ≈ 1.37–1.50 m`) fires in ALL 32 baseline flights — including all 13 **l2r successes** (l2r run 0: 57/85 ticks >3 m off gt; r2l run 31: 68/78). So the phantom is NOT what separates win from loss. The true differentiator is **physical closest approach**: `miss == min_gt_range` for all 32 flights (l2r CPA 0.22–3.34 m *despite the same phantom*; r2l stalls at 5.91–8.11 m). **This makes negative-mining PLAUSIBLY A NULL** (same class as the ADR-0077 range-gate) — it attacks a phantom that is present in the winning direction too.
  - **Leading mechanism for the r2l asymmetry (l2r/r2l are mirror-symmetric BY CONSTRUCTION — `mc_batch.sh:279-307`, verified):** the only symmetry-breaking element is the **body-fixed own-airframe prop-arm phantom at camera-LEFT-down** (`finetuned_seeker.py:63-69` self-mask comment; fires during takeoff/dash before the target is acquirable). Via `--fuse-midcourse` it can drag the cue-guided DASH toward camera-left, which is *toward* the target in l2r but *away* in r2l → r2l CPA stalls. **("NN-only" runs the FULL S2 stack — `--fuse-midcourse --cue-velocity --early-handoff --handoff-cue-gate 8 --dash-unclamp`; it only means "not `--track`.")** DIAGNOSTIC RUNNING: drop `--fuse-midcourse` (pure-cue midcourse) → if r2l recovers, the fusion-of-phantom is the cause (fix = gate midcourse fusion on detection quality / fix the self-mask, NOT mining).
  - **Train/test LEAKAGE caught (Risk 1):** the canonical capture flew the EXACT master-seed-42 r2l seeds (runs 1,3,…,15) and mined their frames → validating the retrain back on master-seed-42 would be flattered. FIX APPLIED: validation moved to UNSEEN master-seed 123, 2-arm paired (v2 vs hardened). Caveat (review): the Gazebo scene + prop-arm phantom are static across seeds, so disjoint seeds test "generalizes across cue/jitter/path draws," not "a new scene" — disclose.
  - **Risk 3 (if the retrain resumes):** `quad_dataset_hardened` is ~36% negatives (under ADR-0040's 40% cap) — but injecting 1436 hard-negs at conf 0.25 can push recall down and regress l2r (ADR-0077 regressed l2r 81→25%). Any validation MUST include l2r + watch positive recall.
  - **Risk 4 (data-quality):** `mc_batch.sh:532-535` coverage regex is broken (interposed `terminal_rejects/…` fields) → CSV `coverage` empty, `breakoff_reason` misleading, `first_det_range_m` mixes phantom (~1.5 m) with real (~15 m). Don't cite those CSV columns; fix the regex. (Pk/miss/direction parse correctly.)
  - **SOLID / review-confirmed (NOT manufactured doubt):** the **41% Pk@2.5 and 81%/0% split are real and correctly computed**, NOT artifacts of start-range geometry (mirror-symmetric), GPU/CPU render, load, or seeds; `direction` is by world-velocity sign (name may read visually reversed, harmless); **miss is the honest physical CPA** (phantom doesn't corrupt scoring); it IS a perception phantom (meas 1.5 m at true 15 m), not a guidance sign error; honesty boundary clean (gt used only offline for labels). **Bottom line: trust the 41% baseline; do NOT trust the mining fix until r2l recovers on HELD-OUT seeds AND l2r holds. Diagnose the --fuse-midcourse mechanism first.**

- **Addendum #10 (2026-07-12, overnight) — `--fuse-midcourse` REFUTED as the r2l cause; the phantom acts via the HANDOFF, and phantom vs target are CO-LOCATED (size-separable) → appearance negative-mining IS the correct discriminator (not a NULL).** Diagnostic per add #9 (drop `--fuse-midcourse`, NN-only, master-seed 42, n=16): **r2l 0/8 (miss median 7.45 m) — IDENTICAL to baseline's 7.43 m; l2r held 88% (7/8).** So the audit's leading mechanism (phantom fused into the midcourse DASH) is WRONG — removing midcourse camera fusion changes r2l nothing. The r2l failure is the phantom triggering the SEEKER HANDOFF: the phantom reads ~1.5 m, satisfies the in-range handoff streak → the interceptor commits to a false close target → CPA stalls ~7.4 m. **KEY refinement:** in r2l the phantom AND the real target are CO-LOCATED (both camera-left — phantom `meas_x ≈ −0.59`, real `≈ −0.42`, per the earlier per-tick split) but differ in SIZE (big phantom box vs small far-target box). So a position/range gate can't separate them (the range-gate already failed, ADR-0077), but an APPEARANCE-trained NN can. **This makes negative-mining the CORRECT discriminator, not the NULL the audit feared** — train the NN to stop firing on the big camera-left phantom while still detecting the small real target. Retrain RESUMED; leakage-free validation on unseen master-seed 123 will settle it (success = r2l recovers + l2r holds; NULL = phantom/target not appearance-separable at 640 → escalate to the 4K/higher-res retrain, ADR-0074). Hypothesis for why l2r wins despite the same phantom: the seeker picks the highest-confidence box; when the real target is well-separated from the phantom (l2r) it can win the handoff, but co-located (r2l) the phantom dominates. Levers now EXHAUSTED except mining + resolution: range-gate (NULL), `--track` (retracted), `--fuse-midcourse` (NULL). NN-only + `--fuse-midcourse` stays the deployed config (l2r 81–88%).

- **⚠️ Addendum #11 (2026-07-12, ~08:00) — THE PIVOT: the mining VALIDATION proved the r2l failure is a GUIDANCE/GEOMETRY ASYMMETRY, NOT the phantom. Every overnight PERCEPTION fix was attacking the wrong thing for r2l.** Leakage-free validation (v2 vs epoch-7 hardened, UNSEEN master-seed 123, NN-only, split by direction):
  - **v2 baseline (unseen):** l2r 7/8 (88%, miss 0.92), r2l 1/8 (12%, miss 7.31), ALL 50%.
  - **hardened (unseen):** l2r 0/8 (miss 2.86), r2l 0/8 (miss 6.62), ALL 0/16.
  - **Decisive per-flight evidence:** the hardened seeker (phantom SUPPRESSED by mining) detects the REAL target EARLY — `first_dash_detection ≈ 15–18 m` (true range) — where v2 detects the PHANTOM first (`≈ 1.5–2 m`). **So the mining SUCCEEDED at its goal (kill the phantom, acquire the real target early). YET r2l still misses ~7 m — hardened 6.62 vs v2 7.31, UNCHANGED.** The phantom's presence/absence does not move r2l. **→ THE PHANTOM IS NOT THE r2l CAUSE.** r2l ~7 m is independent of it; the interceptor fails to convert a clean early r2l detection into an intercept. **r2l is a GUIDANCE/GEOMETRY ASYMMETRY** (the mirror-symmetric l2r crosser intercepts <2.5 m; r2l stalls ~7 m CPA).
  - **The hardened l2r "regression" (88→0 Pk@2.5) is epoch-7 UNDER-TRAINING** (mAP50-95 still climbing at 0.83 → noisy boxes → l2r terminal degraded 0.9→2.5–3.5 m), NOT a detection failure — it acquires at 15 m, EARLIER than v2's late 2 m. More epochs would restore l2r precision, but that's moot: the phantom was never the r2l blocker.
  - **CONCLUSION / PIVOT:** the audit's Risk 2 ("the phantom isn't the differentiator") was RIGHT; my add #10 ("phantom-via-handoff causes r2l") was WRONG — the mining disproved it. All four perception levers (range-gate NULL, `--track` retracted, `--fuse` NULL, mining regressed-but-revealing) failed at r2l because **r2l is a guidance problem.** NEW DIRECTION (the real close-interception lever): find the l2r/r2l guidance asymmetry — a sign/frame-convention issue that is correct for one crossing sense and mirrored-wrong for the other. Suspects: strapdown LOS `λ = ψ + β` (yaw + camera bearing), world→NED mapping (north=world_y, east=world_x; ADR-0013), pro-nav command sign `a=N·Vc·λ̇`, absolute-yaw setpoint direction, terminal breakoff, OR the target's `--orient-to-velocity` bank flipping the silhouette asymmetrically. v2 stays deployed (l2r 88%); hardened NOT adopted (under-trained + r2l unfixed). **Mining byproduct kept:** it DOES suppress the phantom + acquire ~13 m earlier — potentially valuable once trained to saturation, separate from the r2l guidance fix.

- **✅ Addendum #12 (2026-07-12, ~08:20) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND (5-agent workflow, high confidence, DATA-VERIFIED): the r2l failure is a NON-MIRROR WEAVE (a TEST-HARNESS bug), NOT an interceptor/guidance bug. The interceptor+guidance are PROVABLY mirror-symmetric.** A workflow (5 independent angles — trajectory, LOS/pro-nav, world-frame/yaw, geometry, terminal — + synthesis; `wf_r2l_guidance`) settled it:
  - **ROOT CAUSE:** `scripts/m4_target_mover.py:152-161` `_perp_unit()` returns a FIXED left-hand perpendicular `(-vy/speed, vx/speed)`; the weave branch (:224-232) applies the SAME offset schedule both directions. For a +Y crosser (l2r) perp=−X → target pulled TOWARD the interceptor (crosses NEAR, x=6.5−1.91=**4.59 m**); for a −Y crosser (r2l) perp=+X → pushed AWAY (crosses FAR, x=**8.41 m**). `mc_batch.sh` mirrors only start_y+vy (a correct reflection across the downrange axis), so the weave is the ONE element that fails to reflect. The crossing lands at the weave's first quarter-period peak (~1.17 s) → the offset is at its extreme exactly at CPA. **DATA-VERIFIED:** `gt_tag_x@CPA` deterministically pinned at **4.59–5.05 m for all 16 l2r vs 8.32–8.41 m for all 16 r2l** (despite ±1.5 m seed jitter). Line-path batches are near-symmetric — the failure appears ONLY with the weave.
  - **The interceptor + guidance are PROVABLY mirror-symmetric (4 of 5 angles clean):** pro-nav `a=N·Vc·λ̇` is reflection-equivariant (the λ̇ sign-flip and the perp-basis handedness-flip CANCEL; confirmed by ADR-0026 gain 5.000 r²=1.000 BOTH directions); world→NED (north=y/east=x) is a proper rotation applied consistently at every site; yaw setpoints mirror-equivariant; terminal keys only on range magnitude. **r2l is NOT a guidance sign bug** — my add #11 "guidance asymmetry" framing was directionally right (not perception) but the asymmetry is in the TARGET MOVER, not the interceptor.
  - **SECONDARY (a REAL capability gap, separate):** the DASH lead-aim (tpos+vt·t_go) uses a garbage TargetTracker velocity (tgt_vn/ve_hat ≈ ±30 m/s vs true ±12), and the fast FAR r2l crossing sweeps out of FOV → the ENGAGE far-dropout branch (`m4_intercept.py:3136-3138`) commands a dead-stop → the ~7 m stall. Exposed BY the unfair far crossing, not its cause.
  - **HONEST IMPLICATION (important nuance):** the quad's "41% (l2r 81%/r2l 0%)" was substantially a TEST ARTIFACT (r2l target 3.8 m farther at CPA). BUT the billboard's 72/72 (ADR-0064) ran on the SAME buggy weave and PASSED — so the far r2l crossing IS interceptable with good perception; the quad fails it because its weaker perception (FOV loss + garbage tracker velocity on the fast/far crossing) can't. So there are TWO real, now-SEPARABLE facts: (1) the weave is unfairly asymmetric (harness bug), and (2) the quad has a perception/robustness gap on far/fast crossings that the far r2l crossing exposes. **TOP FIX (harness):** `m4_target_mover.py` weave branch × `math.copysign(1.0, vy)` on the offset + lat term → both cross at the NEAR extreme (4.6 m), symmetric; vy>0 (l2r) stays byte-identical (built-in invariance check). Expected: r2l `gt_tag_x@CPA` 8.4→4.6, r2l Pk rises toward l2r. **CONFIRMING:** quad `--path line` A/B (running) → if r2l≈l2r, interceptor exonerated. Do NOT overclaim the copysign fix "solves the quad" — it makes the test FAIR (symmetric); the perception gap on far/fast crossings is a real, separately-documented limitation (the honest portfolio framing). All prior WEAVE Pk comparisons split by direction are confounded by this bug; re-run the key ones on the fixed weave.
  - **⚠️ LINE-TEST UPDATE (immediately after): the weave is an AMPLIFIER, NOT the sole cause.** The zero-code confirmation (quad, `--path line`, symmetric geometry, master-seed 42, v2 NN-only) gave **l2r 4/8 (50%, miss 2.65 m) vs r2l 0/8 (0%, miss 5.76 m)** — r2l STILL fails on perfectly symmetric geometry. So removing the weave helped (r2l miss 7.43→5.76 m) but a RESIDUAL r2l asymmetry persists. This CONTRADICTS the synthesis's "interceptor provably symmetric → weave is the culprit" as a COMPLETE explanation. Reconciliation: the GUIDANCE is symmetric (the workflow's pro-nav/world-frame/yaw/terminal audits stand), but the **markerless PERCEPTION is asymmetric** — the known **ADR-0056** finding: the markerless seeker reads r2l much worse than l2r (a box-center × view-aspect artifact; the AprilTag seeker shows NO such asymmetry). So the r2l failure = weave-amplification (harness bug, fixable) + a residual markerless-perception aspect bias (ADR-0056, the real hard part). The weave-mirror A/B (running) quantifies the weave's share; the residual needs a perception fix (subpixel/centroid bearing — `docs/subpixel_bearing_prereg.md`, or the AprilTag-parity investigation of ADR-0056). Honest net: the "41%" is inflated-hard by the weave bug, but the quad's markerless r2l perception is genuinely weaker — do NOT claim the weave fix alone recovers r2l.
  - **✅ WEAVE-MIRROR A/B RESULT — QUANTIFIED (the r2l arc's bottom line).** `INTERCEPTOR_WEAVE_MIRROR=1` (both directions cross NEAR ~4.6 m; committed default-OFF in `m4_target_mover.py`) vs the baseline buggy weave, n=16 paired master-seed 42: **l2r 88% (miss 0.84 m — INVARIANCE HOLDS, l2r byte-identical) ; r2l miss 7.43 → 3.99 m but STILL 0/8 Pk@2.5.** So the harness weave bug accounted for **~3.4 m of the ~7.4 m** r2l miss (fixed by the flag), but the residual **~4.0 m** keeps r2l at 0% — that residual is the **ADR-0056 markerless-perception aspect bias**. **NET CONCLUSION of the entire r2l investigation:** r2l 0% = (a) an unfair weave [test-harness bug, ~half the miss, FIXED via the default-OFF flag] + (b) a residual markerless r2l perception aspect bias [ADR-0056 — the genuinely hard part]. The GUIDANCE is provably mirror-symmetric (the 5-agent workflow). Even on the FAIR weave the quad aggregate is ~44% (l2r ~88% / r2l still 0%) → **the weave fix is a CORRECTNESS/FAIRNESS win, NOT a Pk recovery**; landing r2l needs a PERCEPTION fix (subpixel/centroid bearing — `docs/subpixel_bearing_prereg.md`, or the ADR-0056 aspect-bias root cause). Levers now fully mapped: range-gate (NULL), `--track` (retracted), `--fuse` (NULL), negative-mining (regressed but proved phantom≠cause), weave-mirror (fixes the harness half). Deployed config untouched; adopting `INTERCEPTOR_WEAVE_MIRROR=1` as the fair-test default + re-running the key portfolio numbers on it is a BUILDER call.

- **✅ Addendum #13 (2026-07-12, fresh session — builder directive "get straight-line then maneuvering intercept working"): NEGATIVE-MINING DEFINITIVELY CLOSED on a CLEAN fully-trained test; the r2l phantom IDENTIFIED as the interceptor's OWN prop blades; and the reason every cheap filter fails is pinned down.**
  - **The add #11 "mining is a NULL" verdict was UNINFORMATIVE — it tested an epoch-7 (under-trained) seeker.** Re-examined its r2l validation flight (`m4_intercept_pronav_20260712T135509Z.csv`): **0 ENGAGE ticks, 0 BREAKOFF, 527 ticks, CPA 7.17 m** — the seeker was too weak to ever hand off, so it never engaged. That is NOT "phantom suppressed, still misses"; it is "never acquired." So the fully-trained test was still owed.
  - **CLEAN fully-trained result (harvested `best.pt` @ ~epoch 49, val mAP50 0.994 / mAP50-95 0.95; leakage-free master-seed 123 — mining flights were seed 42; n=16, NN-only, weave, WEAVE_MIRROR=1):** l2r **0/8 @ 2.73 m** (REGRESSED from v2's ~88%/0.8 m), r2l **2/8 @ 3.29 m** (phantom's catastrophic false-breakoff GONE — one flight 0.35 m clean — but not clean), overall **2/16 = 13% vs v2's ~44%**. So negative-mining KILLS the phantom but the hard-negative injection REGRESSES box precision (add #9 Risk 3, confirmed) → net WORSE. Both directions land ~3 m and SYMMETRIC → independently reconfirms the guidance is mirror-symmetric (add #12). `logs/mc_quad_hardened_s123_weave.csv`. **Negative-mining is not the r2l fix — closed.**
  - **THE PHANTOM IS THE INTERCEPTOR'S OWN PROP BLADES.** Viewed a mined true-negative frame (`quad_hard_negatives/c1_000069_r016.0_neg.png`, 1436/1588 mined frames are true-neg): NO drone present, but the seeker fires a big box on the interceptor's OWN dark propeller blades intruding from the TOP-LEFT/TOP-RIGHT frame edges against the light sky. A fixed body-frame feature (confirms add #9's own-airframe hypothesis). The self-mask (`finetuned_seeker.py`: reject >±30° or within 40 px of the L/R edge) misses it because the blade intrudes ~450 px inward and the seeker boxes the INNER blade tip (bearing < 30°, off-edge).
  - **WHY EVERY CHEAP FILTER FAILS (pinned from flight data — do not re-try these):** (a) **the seeker's range is unreliable** — at the real r2l handoff the TRUE range is 4.0 m but the camera reads `meas_range` **1.57 m**, IDENTICAL to the phantom's 1.5 m at a true 15 m (`m4_intercept_pronav_20260712T191350Z.csv` @ handoff). So a real close target and a far phantom read the SAME range → no range-gate/size-cap can separate them (this is why ADR-0077 range-gate regressed 81→25%, and why a box-size cap fails: phantom min 118 px overlaps real 2-8 m boxes up to 233 px). (b) **the phantom box centers OVERLAP real-target image space** (measured over 989 phantom dets: cx/W p50 0.42, cy/H bimodal 0.2-0.4 & ~0.95 — center-left, not a clean corner) → a position mask can't separate them without masking real targets. Filters tried & failed/closed: range-gate (NULL), --track (retracted), --fuse (NULL), negative-mining (regresses), position-mask & size-cap (overlap, pre-verified this session, not flown).
  - **RECONCILED with add #12 via flight forensics:** the r2l dash UNDER-CLOSES — CPA 3.9 m happens DURING the DASH, then the target recedes (the phantom's post-handoff 1.5 m only causes the breakoff, not the miss). Consistent with add #12's markerless-perception aspect bias + the SECONDARY gap (garbage TargetTracker velocity ±30 vs true ±12, far-FoV dropout dead-stop). AprilTag (clean perception) shows NO r2l asymmetry → the binding constraint is markerless perception QUALITY on the small far/edge-on quad, exactly what the auto-crop attacks.
  - **THE REMAINING HONEST LEVER = AUTO-CROP (ADR-0074).** More pixels-on-target → a reliable box/range AND excludes the body-fixed edge props from a target-centered crop — the only lever that attacks the root (poor resolution) rather than filtering an unfilterable phantom. The crop seeker was ALREADY trained in a prior session (`runs/drone_finetune_quad_crop/best.pt`, val mAP50 0.995) but never exported/validated → exported `drone_finetuned_quad_crop.onnx`, **VALIDATION IN FLIGHT** (`mc_quad_crop_s123_weave`, MARKERLESS_AUTOCROP=1, NN-only, seed 123). Risk: acquisition bootstraps off the last detection / full-frame fallback (add #6) — if the phantom seeds the crop center, acquisition may still misfire; the flight settles it. Tooling committed: `scripts/quad_seeker_arm.sh` (the reusable non-deployment quad-seeker validation arm). Deployed v2 + billboard fallback UNTOUCHED.

- **✅ Addendum #14 (2026-07-12, fresh session cont.): AUTO-CROP validated (preserves l2r, does NOT fix r2l); the r2l root cause pinned to ASPECT-BIASED LATE DETECTION; the dash-lead-velocity hypothesis CLEANLY RULED OUT. r2l is a fundamental markerless-perception limit — every guidance lever is now exhausted.**
  - **AUTO-CROP (ADR-0074) flown** (crop seeker was already trained in a prior session — val mAP50 0.995 — exported `drone_finetuned_quad_crop.onnx`; MARKERLESS_AUTOCROP=1, NN-only, seed 123): weave l2r **6/8 @ 2.24 m** (PRESERVES precision, unlike the hardened seeker's 0/8) / r2l **1/8 @ 3.76 m**; line l2r 2/8 / r2l 0/8 @ 5.26 m. It does NOT fix r2l — the acquisition bootstraps off the last detection / full-frame fallback (add #6), so the phantom still seeds acquisition. r2l floors ~3.3–5.4 m across ALL seekers (v2/hardened/crop). `logs/mc_quad_crop_s123_{weave,line}.csv`.
  - **ROOT CAUSE (flight forensics, r2l vs l2r on the same geometry):** l2r closes to 0.36 m DURING the dash; r2l stalls at ~4 m during the dash then the target recedes. The differentiator is `first_dash_detection_range`: **r2l = 3.64 m** (the seeker does not detect the r2l aspect until it is nearly on top of the target) vs l2r much earlier. Late detection → the interceptor is out of position when it finally acquires → terminal-coast freezes an UNMATCHED velocity ((15.78 E, −2.64 N) racing cross-range while the target flees at −12 N) → escape. This is the ADR-0056 markerless r2l aspect bias (the AprilTag shows NO r2l asymmetry).
  - **DASH-LEAD-VELOCITY hypothesis — BUILT, TESTED, CLEANLY NULL (a real negative, not manufactured doubt):** the DASH lead uses `target_tracker.vel_hat`, and for r2l it collapsed to **−0.6** (vs true −12) — the phantom detections (even when cue-gate-rejected for HANDOFF) still correct the DASH tracker (m4:2349) and the alpha-beta beta-term differentiates garbage. The cue EMITS the correct velocity (s2_cue vy −11.84). Built `--cue-vel-hold` (default-off): re-assert the fresh cue velocity onto the tracker after the camera correction so it stays authoritative. It WORKED at its stated goal — r2l dash `tgt_vn_hat` −0.6 → **−11.7** (clean) — **YET r2l Pk UNCHANGED (0/8, mean paired Δ +0.06 m) and it slightly REGRESSED l2r** (the cue velocity is noisier σ=0.5 than l2r's good camera differentiation). So the lead velocity is DEFINITIVELY NOT the r2l cause — add #12's "garbage tracker velocity" gap is real but not the blocker. Reverted (null + l2r regression; not kept even default-off). One flight flipped 5.0 m→0.03 m under the narrow first variant = geometry luck, not reproduced by the stronger fix.
  - **CONCLUSION — r2l is a FUNDAMENTAL markerless-perception limit (late aspect-biased detection), not a fixable bug.** Every lever is now exhausted: perception (range-gate NULL, --track retracted, --fuse NULL, negative-mining regresses, position/size filters can't separate, auto-crop preserves-l2r-not-r2l) AND guidance (cue-vel-hold cleanly NULL; the 5-agent workflow's pro-nav/frame audit stands). ALL markerless seekers detect the r2l aspect late (aspect appearance is genuinely hard), so no seeker-swap / coarse-to-fine helps — the fix is a fundamentally BETTER SENSOR (higher-res / the parent's real seeker), honesty-gated + a large build. **This is the parent project's whole thesis** (jam-resistant onboard seeker). **The billboard 72/72 both-directions (ADR-0064) is UNAFFECTED and remains the headline "intercept works" result; the realistic quad works l2r (~88% weave) and its r2l is the honestly-scoped perception frontier.** No code adopted; deployed v2 + billboard fallback untouched.

- **✅ Addendum #15 (2026-07-12, Fable review + follow-up): add #14's "fundamental perception limit, needs a better sensor" verdict is OVERSTATED — r2l IS refinable. The binding separator is APPEARANCE (the phantom overlaps the real target in BOTH range and image space, so every spatial/downstream lever is exhausted); a balance-corrected retrain is the remaining lever, LAUNCHED.** Builder asked for an independent Fable feasibility review (he believed it was refinable); Fable was right.
  - **FABLE CAUGHT A REAL GAP (the "head builds, Fable reviews" pattern paying off):** (1) add #14's "aspect detectable only at 3.64 m" is v2-specific and CONTRADICTED by add #11 — the hardened (phantom-suppressed) seeker acquired the real r2l target at **15–18 m**. So the aspect is NOT invisible at 640 px; the phantom just wins box-selection until point-blank. (2) The `--cue-vel-hold` test cleaned only the tracker VELOCITY; the tracker POSITION (m4:2349, camera corrects the dash tracker EVERY detected tick with "CAMERA HAS PRIORITY" over the cue, phantom included) was never protected. (3) The l2r kill is made by the cue-guided DASH, not the camera terminal — so a clean r2l dash should also kill.
  - **TRACKER-CUE-GATE experiment (Fable's #1 lever — apply the handoff gate's camera-position-vs-cue discriminator to the tracker corrections, `--tracker-cue-gate`, built default-off):** n=16, v2, seed 42, weave-mirror. **NULL/regression but DECISIVE:** l2r 7/8→**1/8** (0.94→3.45 m), r2l 0/8→0/8 (4.07→3.52 m) — BOTH directions collapse to a **symmetric ~3.5 m = the CUE-ONLY dash floor**. Mechanism: the gate coasts ALL camera detections (real l2r ones too — cue lag near CPA makes them disagree > gate), so both run cue-only. This PROVES: l2r reaches 0.94 m ONLY because its camera detections are the REAL target; r2l is CAPPED at the ~3.5 m cue floor because its camera "detections" are the phantom, and a gate can only REMOVE the phantom's harm, not ADD the real-target benefit. **Reverted.**
  - **IMAGE-MASK experiment (blank the prop band before the NN — the cheapest phantom-SOURCE removal; props measured in a tight y=0.20–0.34 band):** n=16, v2, seed 42, top-36% blanked. **NULL/regression:** l2r 7/8→**3/8** (0.94→2.80 m, one flight failed), r2l 0/8→0/8 (4.07→4.18 m). Offline it cut phantom firings 62% (4.41→1.69/frame) but not fully (props sweep lower under maneuver). The l2r regression is the DEEPEST finding: masking the top clipped the REAL l2r target too → **the props and the real target OVERLAP in image space**, not just in range → NO spatial separator (image-mask, detection-mask, position-gate) can work. **Reverted.**
  - **CONVERGED ROOT CAUSE + THE REMAINING LEVER:** the phantom (own props) overlaps the real target in range (both read ~1.5 m up close) AND image position (both in the top band) → every spatial + downstream discriminator is genuinely exhausted (range-gate, --track, --fuse, position/size filters, auto-crop, cue-vel-hold, tracker-cue-gate, image-mask — ALL null/regression). The ONLY separator left is **APPEARANCE** — a prop vs a drone look different, and the NN can learn it. The from-scratch hard-negative retrain PROVED appearance works (it suppressed the phantom + acquired the real target at 15–18 m) but REGRESSED PRECISION (unbalanced 36%-neg from-scratch injection). **FIX = balance-corrected retrain (Fable's #2 lever): init from v2 (good precision) + ~15% negatives + gentle lr0 0.002 + 30 ep fine-tune** → suppress the phantom WHILE keeping v2's precision. Built `quad_dataset_rebal` (v2 ∪ 469 subsampled true-neg + 152 pos-with-phantom = 15.7% neg) + `train_daemon_quad_rebal.py` (setsid, in flight). Validate leakage-free on unseen master-seed 123: success = r2l Pk@2.5 recovers toward l2r AND l2r holds. **HONEST FEASIBILITY VERDICT (answering the builder): r2l is NOT a fundamental sensor limit — it is refinable via appearance discrimination; the cheap levers are exhausted but the retrain lever is untried-until-now.** If the balance-corrected retrain ALSO regresses precision or fails to suppress the phantom, THEN the next stop is native-1280 resolution or the camera-mount design fix (props out of FOV) — but not before. Deployed v2 + billboard fallback UNTOUCHED; billboard 72/72 both-ways (ADR-0064) unaffected.

- **✅ Addendum #16 (2026-07-12, builder-directed camera-mount test): moving the seeker camera FORWARD past the own props REMOVES THE PHANTOM AT THE SOURCE (validated!) — but the mount offset needed to clear the props introduces a guidance PARALLAX the code doesn't model, so it needs a lever-arm correction to actually win. The camera-move is the design-correct root fix but NOT the "minimal-effort" fix it first appeared.**
  - **Builder directive:** "if we can extend the camera past the blades with minimal effort, skip the retrain and do that to reduce phantoms." Assessed: the camera pose is 2 lines in PX4's `x500_mono_cam/model.sdf` (`.12 .03 .242` = 12 cm fwd), swappable via the ADR-0068/adaptive_tilt backup→swap→restore pattern (PX4 loads it from a hardcoded path, bypassing GZ_SIM_RESOURCE_PATH).
  - **PHANTOM REMOVED — VALIDATED.** Moved the camera to x=0.40 (past the front rotors ~0.18). Rendered r2l-aspect frames: **the top corners are now EMPTY (no prop blades), the target is still centered/visible.** v2 on those frames fires ONLY the real target (25 px box at 15 m — correct range — vs the old 118–386 px phantom reading 1.5 m). In flight, `first_dash_detection` jumped **3.64 m → 12–15 m in BOTH directions** — the real r2l target is now acquired early (confirms add #15: the aspect was never invisible; the phantom just won box-selection).
  - **BUT THE MOUNT OFFSET BREAKS THE TERMINAL GEOMETRY.** The guidance builds the target as `own_ned + range·LOS` (m4:1154/2350/2826) — it does NOT model a camera lever-arm, so a big camera offset makes the camera-implied position/LOS wrong at close range. Result (v2, seed 42, weave-mirror, n=16): **x=0.40 → l2r 7/8→0/8 (0.94→3.21 m), r2l 0/8 (4.07→3.83 m), overall 0/16** — BOTH directions collapse to the symmetric ~3.5 m CUE-ONLY floor (the parallax makes the real detections cue-inconsistent → gated / mistracked → camera contribution lost). Tried a SMALLER move x=0.28 (props still clear, early-det 14–15 m) → l2r still 3.1 m + 17 handoff-cue-rejects. **The tolerance threshold (stock 0.12 m is fine) is BELOW the ~0.20 m needed to clear the props → NO mount distance both clears the props AND keeps the parallax tolerable.**
  - **NET: the camera-move needs a GUIDANCE LEVER-ARM CORRECTION to work** (transform the camera-anchored LOS/range to the base_link frame using the known camera offset + own yaw — honest own-airframe geometry, no gt). Variants kept: `scripts/experiments/cam_forward{,28}/`.
  - **LEVER-ARM BUILT + TESTED (builder chose this path) → the yaw-only correction is INSUFFICIENT: 0/16.** Added `--cam-fwd-offset-m` (default 0.0 = byte-identical): transforms the camera (range, LOS) into the base_link frame each fresh tick — `target_rel = fwd*(cos psi, sin psi) + range*(cos lambda, sin lambda)`, then `range_g=|.|, lambda=atan2` — applied to the pro-nav LOS/range filters, the dash tracker, the fused track, AND the handoff-cue-gate (honesty: own yaw + a static mount constant, no gt; 44 insertions, AST-pinned default-off). Result (x=0.40, `--cam-fwd-offset-m 0.40`, v2, seed 42, weave-mirror, n=16): **l2r 0/8 @3.23 m (still regressed from 0.94 m), r2l 0/8 @3.96 m, overall 0/16.** The handoff-gate correction did drop r2l's spurious rejects (23→0) and one flight hit 0.66 m, but the fleet did NOT recover. **Why the yaw-only lever-arm falls short:** (a) it ignores the mount's 0.24 m UP-offset and the dash PITCH (~30°), so a residual parallax remains through the pitched terminal; (b) the NN (v2) was trained at the STOCK viewpoint, so at the 0.40 m-forward view its box precision degrades (a moved-camera re-render of the quad dataset is likely needed). **HONEST VERDICT: the camera-move REMOVES THE PHANTOM (validated — the real r2l target is acquired early, both dirs) but does NOT by itself land the intercept; completing it needs a FULL 3-D lever-arm (pitch + up) AND/OR an NN re-train at the moved viewpoint — a bigger build than the yaw-only v1.** The balance-corrected retrain (add #15, appearance fix, no camera/guidance change) remains the lower-surface-area alternative. `--cam-fwd-offset-m` kept as a validated default-off tool. PX4 stock model RESTORED (verified `.12`). Deployed config + billboard 72/72 (ADR-0064) UNTOUCHED.

- **★★ Addendum #17 (2026-07-13) — THE PIVOT (data-verified, clean): PHANTOM-REMOVAL IS A DEAD-END, and the r2l failure is a DASH-LEAD GUIDANCE BUG, not perception. Refutes add #12/#15's perception framing; vindicates add #11.**
  - **CLEAN PAIRED CONTROL (all seed 123, weave-mirror, NN-only) settles the confound:** v2 (phantom PRESENT) = **l2r 8/8 @0.72 m, r2l 0/8 @4.17 m**; rebal (phantom GONE — the balance-corrected retrain WORKED: phantom firings 4.41→**0.00**/frame, box precision IoU 0.97 IDENTICAL to v2) = **l2r 1/8 @2.93 m, r2l 1/8 @3.83 m**. Paired l2r delta rebal−v2 = **+2.14 m WORSE, every flight.** So: (a) seed 123 is NOT hard (v2 gets l2r 8/8 @0.72 m); (b) **removing the phantom REGRESSES l2r** — the phantom was incidentally HELPING l2r (delaying its handoff so the tight cue-DASH makes the kill, not the bearing-floored camera terminal); (c) **r2l floors ~3.8–4.2 m WITH OR WITHOUT the phantom** → the phantom is NOT the r2l cause. **Phantom-removal (camera-move Path A, appearance-retrain Path B) is a NET REGRESSION (2/16 vs v2 8/16) — abandon it.** rebal + camera variants kept as tools; deployed v2 untouched.
  - **ROOT CAUSE (trajectory forensics, v2 seed 123, l2r 0.72 vs r2l 4.17):** both targets cross at the SAME east offset E≈6.5 (mc_batch mirrors N only), so BOTH need a +east dash lead. l2r commands **+east** (cmd_ve +9…+16) → reaches E≈6.5 → intercepts. r2l commands **−east** (cmd_ve −5, −8, **−12**) → drives WEST, away from the target → a ~4 m east gap it never closes. **Mechanism:** the shared `TargetTracker` alpha-beta east-velocity `tgt_ve_hat` SPIKES to ~−26 m/s (true ≈0) from differentiating noisy/aspect-biased camera positions (m4:~2369 corrects the tracker every detected tick); the DASH lead `aim = tpos + vt·t_go` then throws the aim-east far west (6.5 − 26·t_go ≪ 0). Present with v2 (same seeker as the l2r that hits 0.72 m) → **phantom-INDEPENDENT, a guidance/estimator bug.**
