  - **WHY `--cue-vel-hold` (add #14) was NULL is now clear + fixable:** it re-asserted the cue velocity onto the tracker but was tested WITH the phantom (v2) confounding it, and the DASH lead also runs a FUSE path. The clean cue emits ve≈+0.35 (correct); making the cue velocity AUTHORITATIVE for the dash lead across ALL paths (or rejecting the tracker-velocity spikes) should straighten the r2l east lead. **NEXT (guidance, cheap-candidate): fix the dash-lead east velocity → re-test with v2 (l2r-preserving).** If it lands r2l while v2 holds l2r 8/8, that is the fix — no perception change. This is the first cleanly-guidance-rooted r2l lever; the ~10 perception levers are all closed.
  - **Addendum (2026-07-13) — `--dash-lead-cue-vel` BUILT + TESTED → NULL for r2l; the root is deeper (the fused target POSITION, i.e. the aspect-biased BEARING).** Fed the clean HELD cue velocity to BOTH dash-lead paths (raw fallback AND the fuse-path fallback; `_vt_fb`, default-off, reverted). Result (v2 seed 123): l2r stays tight (0.11–0.93 m — overriding the fallback path with the cue velocity even slightly IMPROVES l2r), but **r2l NOT fixed (~4 m).** Forensic on the r2l dash WITH the fix: cmd_ve DID improve (median −8 → **+4.4**, now commanding east) — **yet `own_E` stays ~0.5 m** (the interceptor commands east but does not move east): the huge +north command (chasing the target's far-north start N≈14) dominates the velocity budget, AND the reconstructed aim direction (should be ~mostly-east) does not match the actual command — because the **fused target-POSITION east is wrong**, driven by the r2l aspect's biased camera **bearing** (ADR-0056). So the r2l dash under-commits east because the dash AIM is corrupted by the aspect-biased bearing — a PERCEPTION bearing-quality root after all, not the lead velocity (which is now clean). **NET after ~12 levers:** quad-r2l is a genuinely hard, well-characterized floor: the r2l camera BEARING is aspect-biased (ADR-0056) → the fused dash aim under-commits east → the dash under-closes (CPA in the bearing-floored camera terminal ~3.8 m). Perception-bearing subpixel fixes were NULL (ADR-0071); phantom-removal regresses l2r (add #17); no guidance-lead fix reaches it. m4 reverted to HEAD (the 3-D lever-arm `--cam-{fwd,left,up}-offset-m` kept as a validated tool). **HONEST portfolio position: the billboard 72/72 both-directions (ADR-0064) is the headline; the realistic quad works l2r ~88% and its r2l is a rigorously-characterized markerless-bearing limit — a strong negative-results arc, exactly the perception gap the parent project's better onboard seeker exists to close.**
  - **add #18 (2026-07-15) — THE REAL-BUILD PIVOT: `--coded-dash` architecture BUILT + a PAIRED A/B that partly REFUTES add #17's "no fix reaches r2l".** Builder is building the real interceptor (docs/hardware_order_list.md); its operating model is a CODED open-loop DASH → CAMERA-ONLY terminal, with **NO ground-sensor cue and NO fused mid-course dash** — so the entire cue/FusedTrack machinery add #13–#17 debugged is *absent on the hardware*. Fable's real-build review said to test the flight architecture before spending more perception levers. Built `--coded-dash` (m4: open-loop dash at a COLLISION-LEAD heading solved from the pre-flight target kinematics — a human/ground-cue-programmed constant, no live gt — then the UNCHANGED camera-only ENGAGE terminal; the whole cue stack dropped). Ran it against the EXACT paired baseline (`mc_quad_v2_s123_weave.csv` vs `mc_coded_dash_qv2_weave.csv`, quad_v2, master-seed 123, weave, |x0|=6.5, |y0|≈14.5, n=8/dir — per-flight geometry verified byte-identical). The CUE-GUIDED arm is the full adopted deployment config (`quad_seeker_arm.sh`: `--dash-speed 16 --early-handoff --cue-velocity --fuse-midcourse --handoff-cue-gate 8` + the realistic degraded S2 cue: datum-bias 0.5 m, markov dropout, latency jitter, vel-sigma 0.5); CODED-DASH holds dash-speed 16 + `--dash-unclamp` but drops EVERY cue flag — so this is a fair *architecture* A/B (deployed-sim vs real-hardware), not a single-knob ablation:

    | arm | dir | engaged | clean | Pk@2.5m | miss mean | range |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | CUE-GUIDED | l2r | 8/8 | 8/8 | **8/8** | **0.61** | 0.26–1.13 |
    | CUE-GUIDED | r2l | 8/8 | 3/8 | **0/8** | **3.97** | 3.25–4.27 |
    | CODED-DASH | l2r | 8/8 | 7/8 | **8/8** | **1.37** | 1.13–1.58 |
    | CODED-DASH | r2l | 8/8 | 8/8 | **5/8** | **2.68** | 2.11–3.72 |

    **RESULT (honest, corrects the interim "0/8→8/8" over-claim I committed mid-run):** the cue-guided r2l was NEVER a no-engagement NULL — it *engaged* 8/8 but at 3.25–4.27 m, i.e. **0/8 inside the 2.5 m Pk gate** (that IS the "r2l 0%" of the whole saga: the bearing-floored ~3.8 m terminal of add #17). **Dropping the fused dash (coded-dash) LIFTS r2l 0/8→5/8 @Pk and pulls the mean 3.97→2.68 m (−1.3 m).** This PARTLY REFUTES add #17's "no fix reaches it": add #17's own mechanism (the fused dash aim, corrupted by the aspect-biased bearing, under-commits east) was real, and *removing that fused dash entirely* — not a lead-velocity patch — recovers ~1.3 m. The RESIDUAL r2l 2.68 vs l2r 1.37 m gap is the terminal bearing aspect-bias (ADR-0056), the honest remaining lever. **Cost:** l2r regresses 0.61→1.37 m mean (still 8/8 @Pk) — the cue genuinely HELPED l2r; coded-dash trades l2r's sub-meter excellence for r2l robustness and EQUALIZES the directions (l2r/r2l gap 6.5×→2×; combined Pk@2.5m 8/16→**13/16**). **This is the number that matters for the hardware** (no cue exists on it). Commits: `--coded-dash` + collision-lead heading + `scripts/coded_dash_arm.sh`; portable `flight/` core extracted (geometry + `collision_lead_heading`, 11 tests). NEXT: dash-heading-error / target-pos-error robustness sweeps (how wrong can the open-loop aim be and still acquire — the real-build question), then wire m4→flight/ and port the Pi/Hailo path.
  - **add #18b (2026-07-15) — ROBUSTNESS: coded-dash tolerates a ±15° open-loop aim error (16/16 still acquire).** The real dash is hand-programmed, so the aim carries operator error — the viability question. Added `--dash-heading-err-deg` / `--dash-target-err-{n,e}` (default 0 = byte-identical) and re-flew the paired weave/seed-123/quad_v2 arm at +15° heading error (`mc_coded_dash_qv2_weave_herr+15.csv`, n=8/dir):

    | arm | dir | acquire | Pk@2.5m | miss mean | Δmean vs 0° |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | +15° | l2r | 8/8 | 8/8 | 2.33 | +0.96 (degrades, stays in gate) |
    | +15° | r2l | 8/8 | **7/8** | **1.60** | **−1.08 (IMPROVES)** |

    **Every flight still ACQUIRES at +15°** — the architecture is robust to a large aim error, strong evidence the hand-programmed dash concept is viable. The signed +15° biases r2l's aim EAST, which *compensates* the r2l bearing under-commit-east (Pk 5/8→7/8, mean 2.68→1.60 m) while mildly loosening l2r (still 8/8 @Pk) — so combined Pk@2.5m actually RISES 13/16→15/16. This independently CONFIRMS the r2l residual is an east-aim deficit (add #17/#18 mechanism) and exposes a deliberate east-bias as a genuine r2l lever (asymmetric — would cost l2r). NEXT: bound the tolerance with a larger error (+30° breaks l2r?), then a target-position-error sweep.
  - **add #18c (2026-07-15) — the FULL heading-error tolerance envelope (48 flights, 0°/+15°/+30°, paired weave/seed-123/quad_v2):**

    | heading err | l2r acquire | l2r Pk@2.5 | l2r miss | r2l acquire | r2l Pk@2.5 | r2l miss |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 0° | 8/8 | 8/8 | 1.37 | 8/8 | 5/8 | 2.68 |
    | +15° | 8/8 | 8/8 | 2.33 | 8/8 | 7/8 | 1.60 |
    | +30° | 8/8 | **0/8** | 3.25 | 8/8 | **8/8** | **0.78** |

    Three clean conclusions for the real build: **(1) ACQUISITION is robust to ≥30° aim error — all 48 flights acquired and engaged** regardless of how far off the open-loop dash pointed (the camera terminal locks once the crossing target enters frame; the hand-programmed dash needs only to point roughly right). **(2) l2r's Pk tolerance is ~15–20°** — in-gate through +15°, out by +30° (miss walks 1.37→2.33→3.25 m, a tight 3.20–3.29 cluster = a consistent over-lead, not a scatter). **(3) r2l improves MONOTONICALLY with a clockwise (east) aim bias — 2.68→1.60→0.78 m, Pk 5/8→7/8→8/8** — so the r2l residual is a *systematic, fully-correctable east-aim deficit* (the aspect-biased r2l bearing under-reads the east LOS; the terminal under-commits east; a matching east correction erases it), NOT a stochastic perception floor. **r2l LEVER (real, not yet built):** a deliberate east-ward bias applied ONLY on the r2l aspect (keyed on the known crossing direction, or better on the aspect-biased bearing itself, ADR-0056) recovers r2l to sub-meter without touching l2r. This is the honest remaining lever for the residual; a blind global heading bias trades l2r for r2l (this geometry's +15° nets best, 15/16, but is geometry-specific). Data: `logs/mc_coded_dash_qv2_weave_herr+{15,30}.csv`, `scripts/coded_dash_summary.py`.
    - *LOS-bias diagnostic (2026-07-15, frame-audited & resolved):* a first pass was confounded by a frame error (compared the OPTICAL `bearing_rad` to a WORLD-frame gt); the frame audit found `gt_cam`/`gt_tag` are the camera's and target's absolute WORLD (ENU) positions, so the correct gt LOS is `atan2(tag−cam east, north)` compared to the measured inertial `lambda_deg`. Corrected, over detected ticks (excluding the close-range/terminal-freeze ENGAGE ticks, where atan2 noise dominates and is unreliable): **l2r mean LOS error −0.03° (accurate); r2l mean +4.63° EAST of truth** (r2l−l2r ≈ +4.7°). So there IS a systematic r2l LOS east-bias — corroborating ADR-0056 and the flight-level east-bias direction — but it is **modest (~4.6°) and noisy (per-flight −18°…+32°), NOT a large clean single constant.** Implication: the demonstrated r2l improvement path is the COARSE dash-level east-bias (add #18b/c, which works on the open-loop dash commit, not by correcting a clean terminal bias); a static ~4.6° terminal-LOS correction is a modest, noisy secondary lever, not a dramatic fix. The real close remains a genuine perception improvement of the aspect-biased bearing (ADR-0056/0071 territory), which prior subpixel work found hard.
  - **add #18d (2026-07-15) — Fable rec #1 tested → NULL: the phantom-free `rebal` seeker does NOT ship.** The Fable review (2026-07-15) predicted that under coded-dash (no cue), the phantom-free `rebal` retrain should now WIN, since the "rebal regresses l2r" verdict (add #17) was measured under the cue architecture where the phantom incidentally helped by delaying handoff. Re-A/B'd rebal vs quad_v2 under coded-dash (paired, quad seed-123 weave, n=8/dir). **RESULT: rebal FAILS in flight** — coverage 0.000–0.019 across ALL 16 flights, every one ABORTED "no camera acquire within 8 s". The tempting "16/16 Pk@2.5" was a MIRAGE: with no acquisition the coded-dash never hands to the camera terminal, so the recorded miss is the OPEN-LOOP DASH's CPA (the collision-lead dash alone lands ~1–2.4 m for this geometry), NOT a camera-guided intercept. The `clean=0`/`breakoff=python_exit_1` flag flagged it — the Pk headline alone would have shipped a blind seeker (methodology: statistics-before-verdicts, verify the flag). **Mechanism:** the balance-corrected retrain killed the own-prop phantom by OVER-SUPPRESSING real-target recall in flight — the exact "aced its own frame-eval (precision = v2), regressed in flight" pattern as the v3 NULL (ADR-0061). **DECISION: `quad_v2` remains the deployed/ship seeker** (flight coverage ~0.23–0.33, real intercepts, 13/16 @Pk). The own-prop phantom is therefore NOT solved by an appearance retrain; the hardware mitigation is **prop-clearance geometry (BOM §0②) + the no-cue handoff hardening** (streak range-consistency + pre-flight range window). Built `--dash-crossing-bias-deg` (auto-keys the bias SIGN on the crossing direction = the 2-D cross of the dash heading and --target-vel, a pre-flight constant → r2l +bias / l2r −bias in one config, no gt). **VALIDATED on 2 seeds (123+777, n=16/dir):** r2l median **0.72 m, 9/16 @1 m, best 0.37 m (contact)** — a big gain off 3.46 m, best flights make contact but at the EDGE of kill range, not reliable; l2r acq-limited (8/16 lock), median 1.50 m. **The GUIDANCE/aim is largely solved; the residual is ACQUISITION** (l2r) + terminal bearing quality (r2l).   - **add #18g (2026-07-15) — RETRACTION-GRADE CORRECTION (a Fable adversarial verification broke add #18e/f's headline).** The builder asked Fable to check the "guidance solved / phantom hardware-gated / stop sim" claim. It found the "camera-guided r2l kill" is a MIRAGE one level up from add #18d's rebal mirage — and the correction is load-bearing for R4–R7, so it is recorded here in full:
    - **The sub-meter r2l is OPEN-LOOP DASH BALLISTICS, not camera guidance.** Control proof: the range-gate arm (`..._xbias30_rgate4_s123.csv`) NEVER engaged the camera terminal (0 ENGAGE ticks, all flights) yet scored r2l **7/7 <1 m, median 0.54 m — as good as / better than** the "camera-guided" xbias30 arms (0.49–1.26 m). Per-tick forensics: in the sub-meter flights ENGAGE begins only 0.1–1.1 s pre-CPA on a PHANTOM streak, and the pre-CPA ENGAGE detections are ~all phantom (LOS error ±120–140°); pro-nav on a body-fixed phantom ≈ fly straight ≈ continue the dash (a no-op). The flights that DID get a few real detections missed WORSE (1.08–1.80 m). **No camera-tracked terminal kill on the 3D quad exists anywhere in this dataset** — the crossing-bias produced a well-aimed *unguided flyby*, not a camera-guided intercept. (Method fix add #18g: `coded_dash_summary.py` now requires gt-CONSISTENT ENGAGE detections; the old "any ENGAGE detection = camera-guided" audit let the mirage through — `coded_dash_summary.py`.)
    - **The range-overlap claim (#18f) is FALSE; the real wall is different.** Real detections separate cleanly from phantoms in implied range (real median 18.7 m, 94% ≥3 m; phantom median 1.6 m). #18f's min-4 gate aborted everything because, restricted to the APPROACH (CODED_DASH, gt>8 m), the detector produced **2 real vs 1187 phantom detections across 63 flights — there was nothing real to acquire, gated or not.** So (i) the "r2l 16/16 vs l2r 8/16" asymmetry is phantom-streak behavior, not target acquisition; (ii) **the phantom is the false-handoff HAZARD, not the acquisition BLOCKER** — "prop-clearance fixes acquisition" is now an UNTESTED assumption, not a conclusion; (iii) the true wall is **approach-aspect / high-closing-speed detector recall ≈ 0** (the same detector sees the RECEDING target fine at 20–30 m), pointing at blur/aspect → the real-data detector (R6), not the mount, may be the critical path.
    - **WHAT SURVIVES:** the crossing-bias as an OPEN-LOOP AIM CALIBRATION is real (baseline r2l 3.46 → dash 0.54 m); the "drop the corrupted fused dash" architecture gain is real; "stop sweeping bias angles" is correct (geometry-fragile: s123 vs s777 r2l 0.52 vs 1.26). **RETRACTED:** "guidance solved", "camera-guided r2l kill", "camera terminal finds the target once it enters frame" (`real_build_coded_dash.md`), and "phantom is the l2r acquisition blocker → hardware prop-clearance fixes it".
    - **NEXT (Fable-ranked, before any hardware inherits this):** ① **camera-forward × coded-dash** — re-fly line9 crossing-bias with the phantom removed at source (`scripts/experiments/cam_forward/` moved-camera model + `--cam-fwd-offset-m 0.40`; add #16 validated it removes the phantom and jumps first-detection to 12–15 m, and it only failed under the *cue* architecture coded-dash lacks) — the sim of the exact prop-clearance hardware: does it yield APPROACH acquisition + a genuine camera-tracked <1 m kill, or is recall-at-aspect the wall? ② dash-speed ladder scored on APPROACH-phase real recall (gt-consistent). ③ `MARKERLESS_NN_CONF 0.25→0.10` + min-3 gate (recall threshold-limited?).
  - **add #18h (2026-07-16) — camera-forward × coded-dash FLOWN (the decisive test): prop-clearance ≠ acquisition fix; the real-data detector is the critical path.** Swapped in the camera-forward PX4 model (add #16, camera x=0.40 past the front rotors, phantom out of FOV; safe auto-restore via `scripts/cam_forward_run.sh`, model verified restored) + `--cam-fwd-offset-m 0.40`, re-flew line9 crossing-bias seed 123. Results (gt-consistent audit): **(1) the phantom is REMOVED** — approach-phase phantom detections 1187 (with phantom) → **1** (camera-forward). Confirms add #16. **(2) But APPROACH RECALL is still ~0** — **0 real target detections at gt-range >8 m in either direction** even with the phantom gone; the detector simply does not see the *approaching* target at range (aspect/blur/size), exactly Fable's diagnosis. **(3) So removing the phantom does NOT create camera guidance.** r2l's real detections appear only at gt-range **1.2–4.6 m (at contact), 0–2 pre-CPA** — too late to guide — and one r2l flight hit **0.32 m with ZERO real ENGAGE detections** (pure dash). The tight r2l misses (~0.29 m median) are the well-aimed crossing-bias DASH BALLISTICS, not camera acquisition. **(4) l2r still fails** (0/8 real ENGAGE). **CONCLUSION (endorses the #18g retraction):** the prop-clearance mount (BOM §0②) is still worth it — it removes the phantom = the false-handoff hazard — but it is **NOT** the acquisition fix; the binding wall is approach-aspect detector recall, so the **REAL-DATA DETECTOR (R6) is the critical path**, not the mount. A camera-forward *dash-only control* (forced no handoff, `--coded-dash-acquire-range-min 10`) CONFIRMS it airtight: **r2l dash-only median 0.30 m ≈ with-terminal 0.29 m (Pk@0.5 6/8 vs 8/8), 0/8 vs 7/8 engaged** — the camera terminal adds essentially NOTHING; the sub-meter r2l is entirely open-loop dash ballistics. `logs/mc_coded_dash_qv2_line9_camfwd_*.csv`. **FINAL SIM VERDICT (whole coded-dash arc): the sim validated the guidance ARCHITECTURE (coded dash → camera terminal handoff) and the open-loop AIM (crossing-bias), and it has now precisely localized the one make-or-break unknown for the hardware — approach-aspect detector RECALL, which is ~0 in sim and must be measured on real APPROACHING targets (R5 as an approach pass, not a hover) and fixed with a real-data detector (R6). No camera-guided intercept of the 3D quad is achievable in this sim; that is a detector problem, not a guidance or mount problem.**
  - **add #18i (2026-07-16, autonomous build→check→next loop) — conf-threshold NULL + the wall quantified honestly.** Two follow-ups characterizing the approach-recall wall: **(a) `MARKERLESS_NN_CONF 0.25→0.10` did NOT recover approach recall** — top-1 real-detection rate stayed ~0 (0.1%) while the phantom rate DOUBLED to 44-47% (`logs/mc_coded_dash_qv2_line9_conf10_rgate3_s123.csv`). *Honestly scoped (Fable round-2 check):* this is strong evidence AGAINST a confidence-threshold wall, but the seeker logs only the top-1 box, so it can't fully exclude phantom-masking (a real detection above 0.10 hidden behind a higher-scoring phantom) — the offline all-box probe (next) settles it. **(b) `approach_recall.py` built + corrected** to measure real-target (gt-consistent) detection rate vs range, **closing-only** (a first version counted post-CPA RECEDING ticks as "approach" and inflated it — the detector sees a receding target far better). Corrected TRUE approaching recall (line9): detector sees the target to ~10 m (17% @8-10 m) but **0% beyond 10 m** → approach recall **0.8%** ≥8 m, CONSISTENT across configs (weave 0.8%, camera-forward 0.0% — phantom removed, still 0). So the acquisition envelope is ~≤10 m against a 9 m/s crosser — nowhere near enough terminal time. This is the wall the real-data detector must beat, now measurable.
  - **add #18j (2026-07-16) — resolution-vs-aspect probe: the wall is substantially POINTING/FoV + resolution, NOT purely aspect — a SIGNAL (thin data) that reframes R5/R6.** Captured approach frames of the 3D quad from a coded-dash flight (`scripts/seeker/capture_quad_approach.sh`, capture_flight_frames rides along → frames + gt boxes) and scored 4 arms with ALL boxes ≥ conf 0.05 (`scripts/seeker/resolution_probe.py`, closes the #18i top-1 hole). **Two things the capture itself revealed:** (i) only ~33 frames total had the target IN VIEW; the approaching in-view frames are **3–11 m, then NONE until 37 m+ (the receding target)** — so beyond ~11 m the coded-dash aim (pointing at the LEAD point) leaves the target off-boresight, OUT OF THE FoV. So the "0 detection >8 m" wall (#18h/i) conflated *not-in-view (pointing/FoV)* with *not-detected* — a real metric confound in `approach_recall.py`. (ii) On the in-view approaching band (3–12 m, n=17): **a) v2@640 47%, b) v2@native-gt-crop 59%, c) crop-model@crop 71%, d) aim/centre-crop (deployable) 6%.** READS: the detector DOES fire on the in-view target (47–71%) → **NOT a pure aspect/appearance wall**; the crop HELPS (47→71%) → **resolution is a real lever (foveated crop)**; but the deployable aim-centred crop is **6%** because the target is off-boresight. **REFRAMED conclusion (supersedes "only real data fixes it" from #18h):** the binding walls are **(1) POINTING/FoV — keep the approaching target in view/near boresight (wider real ~100° FoV, aim strategy, or camera tracking); (2) RESOLUTION — foveated crop; (3) real-data detector (aspect) as a secondary/quality lever.** **CAVEAT (mirage discipline):** n=17 in-view frames is THIN and the per-bin rates are noisy — this is a SIGNAL, not a firm flip; confirm with a fuller capture that holds the target in view (a pursuit/target-tracking pass, or the wider-FoV camera) before R5/R6 commit. `scripts/seeker/data/quad_approach/`.
    - **CORRECTION (add #18j-fix, Fable round-3 check — the reframe above was OVERSTATED, my 3rd mirage this session, now caught before it steered R5/R6):** the probe's hit criterion was BACKWARDS (`resolution_probe.py:box_hits_point` tested whether the gt POINT fell inside the padded DETECTED box, so a giant ground-shadow blob CONTAINING the target counted as a hit — a 376×368 blob vs a 60×60 gt target scored as a "hit"). Fixed to `box_hits_gt` (detected-box centre inside the gt box AND size-matched within 3×). Re-run (same frames): **8-10 m 33% for ALL FOUR arms (a=b=c), 10-12 m 0% for all** — the native crop does NOT help at the wall band; the 47→71% "crop lever" was ≤6 m edge-clipped close targets. So: **(1) RESOLUTION is NOT the lever at the wall band** (crop = full-frame at 8-12 m) — retract that claim; **(2) real-data/aspect is NOT demoted** — at 8-12 m the in-view target fails detection regardless of resolution (a 61-px in-frame 9 m target got ZERO boxes from all arms incl. the crop model), so #18h's appearance wall SURVIVES and the real-data detector remains the critical path; **(3) POINTING/FoV is real but the MECHANISM is wrong** — the target exits VERTICALLY (enters frame top ~11 m, exits bottom ~3 m) = NOSE-DOWN DASH PITCH, not the lead-aim (30° lead is well inside ±50° HFOV); and the >12 m "wall" bins are 75-87% TAKEOFF ticks (grounded camera), so the wall DECOMPOSES: **>12 m = not-in-view (dash pitch + takeoff pointing → an UP-TILT / adaptive-tilt fix, #46/ADR-0065); 8-12 m = in-view but detector fails (aspect/appearance/ground-background → real data).** Range never exceeded ~16.7 m (nothing established 17-25 m; the "37 m+" frames were post-flight statics). `approach_recall.py`'s pre-CPA cut is correct but not phase-aware (its >14 m bins mostly measure a grounded takeoff camera — a residual caveat). **Net honest state: real-data detector (aspect, 8-12 m) + camera up-tilt/adaptive-tilt (pointing, >12 m) are the two levers; resolution/foveated-crop is NOT supported at the wall. n=17 still thin — the up-tilt capture (next) gives the real curve.**

## ADR-0077 — PHANTOM RANGE-PLAUSIBILITY GATE (r2l phantom fix, 2026-07-12 overnight): reject a markerless camera detection whose implied range grossly disagrees with the expected range (S2 cue pre-handoff / alpha-beta r_hat post-handoff). Env `MARKERLESS_PHANTOM_RANGE_GATE`, DEFAULT-OFF, byte-identical. Direct fix for the ADR-0076 r2l 0/16 failure (phantom reads 1.5 m at 15 m true).

- **Context.** ADR-0076 add #5/#6: the quad's 41% Pk is a directional split — l2r 81%, r2l 0/16 — and r2l fails because the NN locks a big FALSE box (phantom) reading `meas_range≈1.5 m` while the true range is 15–20 m (43/78 detected ticks >3 m off gt). Guidance chases it → 7 m miss. The phantom is distinguishable by RANGE.
- **The gate (`scripts/m4_intercept.py`).** Pure predicate `phantom_range_reject(meas_range, expected, lo, hi)` — reject if `meas_range < lo*expected` or `> hi*expected` (defaults lo=0.35, hi=2.8; 1.5 vs 15 = 0.10× → rejected). `expected` = the S2 cue range PRE-handoff (only if the cue is FRESH — reuses the ADR-0059 staleness check so a jammed cue can't reject a real closing target), else the alpha-beta smoothed camera range `r_hat` POST-handoff, else (warmup) no gate (acquisition never blocked). Inserted in the tick loop BEFORE the filters/guidance see the detection; a rejected tick is treated as no-detection; `phantom_range_rejects` counter in the summary line. Env-toggle `MARKERLESS_PHANTOM_RANGE_GATE` (default 0 = OFF, byte-identical — 118 insertions / 0 deletions, AST-pinned).
- **Honesty.** Inputs are the cue (legal pre-handoff, already feeds mid-course) + the camera's own `r_hat` (camera-only) — NO `gt_*`. Honesty static-scan + 150 tests pass; 17 dedicated unit tests (phantom rejected, real passed, warmup/off byte-identical, honesty AST scans).
- **Decision.** Committed default-OFF. To be A/B'd overnight on the r2l failure (v2 640 seeker, NN-only, `MARKERLESS_PHANTOM_RANGE_GATE=1`, same seeds) — success = r2l Pk recovers toward l2r's 81%. This is the DIRECT phantom fix (independent of the auto-crop, which attacks the same r2l phantom by cropping out the background — two shots). Conservative gross-outlier bounds, fully reversible.
- **Date.** 2026-07-12.

- **Addendum (2026-07-12) — ADR-0077 range-gate A/B: FAILED (NULL, stays default-OFF).** v2 640 NN-only + `MARKERLESS_PHANTOM_RANGE_GATE=1`, n=16: **l2r REGRESSED 81% → 2/8 (25%), r2l unchanged 0/8, overall 2/16 (worse than the 41% baseline).** The gate fired heavily (13–26 rejects/flight) but rejected REAL detections. Root cause: (1) the box-width range (`meas_range`) is too NOISY to gate on (real detections' implied range swings widely); (2) post-handoff a phantom SEEDS the alpha-beta `r_hat` wrong, so the gate then rejects the true closing detections that disagree with the phantom-seeded expected — it reinforces the phantom. So range-plausibility is the wrong discriminator. Kept default-OFF (reversible). The r2l phantom fix is now the AUTO-CROP (crop out the phantom background) and/or NEGATIVE-MINING (hard-negative the phantom-triggering region — likely the own-airframe/background at the r2l attitude; ADR-0038 lever). Investigating the phantom's frame location next to pick between them.
